# WBS Progress Tracker

Source of truth for WBS execution status. `BrewLedger_WBS_Dictionary.md`
stays an unedited, faithful copy of the dictionary — all status lives here.

**Status values:** `not started` / `in progress` / `blocked` / `needs review`
/ `done` / `pre-existing (adapt, do not scaffold)`.

**Update rule (writer/reviewer separation, mirrors `.claude/agents/WORKFLOW.md`):**
the implementing leg (`engineer`, or `architect` for Pattern A entries) may
only set Status to `needs review` and fill in `Implemented by` + `Commit
ref`. Only the reviewing leg (`qa_engineer` or `redline_reviewer`) may set
Status to `done` and fill in `Reviewed by`. Never self-certify.

## Cross-cutting fix (2026-08) — Owner Console shared shell was silently non-compliant

Found by the user, comparing the live running app against `/design/Owner
Console.html` and `/design/Console Reports.html` side-by-side across several
rounds. Every already-"done" console screen (WBS 4.3, 4.4, 5.8, 5.9, 6.5,
7.1, 7.5, 7.6) was affected, because the defect lived in the shared
shell/layout, not in any one screen. Matters because "done" for those
entries was certified against the screen's own content, not the scaffold it
renders inside — the scaffold was never built at all in one case (Fix 2) and
silently wrong in the others.

1. **`.oc`/`.oc-main` base scaffold CSS was never ported** from
   `/design/Owner Console.html`'s `<style>` block into
   `packages/ui/src/components.css` — confirmed by grep, zero hits before
   this fix. `.oc-body` (used by `DashboardClient`, `PnlPage`,
   `ProfitPerDishPage` already) was **also** completely absent. Ported the
   base rules plus the `@media(min-width:1280px)` slice verbatim (not
   `.oc--auth`/`.oc-total`, out of scope). This is why every screen rendered
   single-column/off-center at desktop widths with no sidebar.
2. **`NavShell` existed but was never rendered anywhere, and had no real
   routing.** `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/layout.tsx` rendered zero
   chrome. `NavShell.tsx` now uses `next/link` + `usePathname()` (derives
   its own active tab; a caller can still override `active` for the
   fidelity gallery, which renders outside any router context) and is
   rendered from `layout.tsx` wrapping `{children}` in `.oc`/`.oc-main`.
   `settings` routes to `/console/settings/store` (no index route exists —
   flagged for `redline_reviewer` as the most sensible default, not a
   spec value). `badge` (unseen order count) intentionally left unset — WBS
   5.8's unseen-order mechanism (`orders/unseen.ts`) is client-side, scoped
   to `InboxClient`'s own realtime feed, not cheaply available at this
   server layout. Known follow-up, not silently dropped.
3. **`MetricCard` always rendered money to 2 decimals**; the prototype
   (`console-reports.js`'s own `B()` helper) shows integers — "฿3,240" not
   "฿3,240.00". Fixed at the `MetricCard` call site only
   (`decimals={0}` passed to `MoneyValue`); `MoneyValue`'s own default stays
   2, flagged as a separate open question for `redline_reviewer`. Broke two
   pre-existing assertions in `DashboardClient.test.tsx` that expected the
   old 2-decimal string ("324.00"/"118.00") — those are now correct per the
   fix and need `qa_engineer` to update, not revert.
4. **No sub-navigation within the Reports section.** Built
   `apps/console/src/components/ReportsSubNav.tsx` (ports `.oc-subnav` from
   `/design/Console Reports.html`, added to `components.css`), rendered at
   the top of `/console/inventory`, `/console/reports/pnl`,
   `/console/reports/profit-per-dish`. **Only 3 of the prototype's 7 `TABS`
   entries have a real route today** — สแกนบิล (WBS 6.1), ตรวจบิล (WBS 6.4),
   รายการเดินบัญชี (GAP-1), เปรียบเทียบ (WBS 7.7) are not built and were
   deliberately omitted rather than linked dead. Extend `TABS` in that file
   as each lands.

5. **No sub-navigation within the Settings section**, same defect as Fix 4
   but for `/console/settings/*` + `/console/menu`. Built
   `apps/console/src/components/SettingsSubNav.tsx` (same `.oc-subnav`
   pattern as `ReportsSubNav`, no new CSS needed — already ported in Fix 4),
   rendered at the top of `/console/settings/store`, `/console/menu`,
   `/console/settings/payments`, `/console/settings/link`,
   `/console/settings/subscription`. Tab order/labels/hrefs sourced from
   `/design/console-setup.js`'s `SETUP` array (line 23) and cross-checked
   against `docs/design/screen_inventory.md`. `เมนู` intentionally points at
   `/console/menu`, not a `/console/settings/menu` route — confirmed by
   `screen_inventory.md`'s own separate top-level entry for it; the
   `pathname === tab.href` exact-match logic (same as `ReportsSubNav`) means
   it can't spuriously highlight or get highlighted by any `/console/settings/*`
   page. **`/console/settings/notifications` (การแจ้งเตือน) has no page.tsx yet**
   — only `docs/design/screen_inventory.md` documents the route (owned by
   WBS 5.8, which built the push-subscription flow inline in the order
   inbox rather than as a dedicated settings screen), no file exists under
   `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/settings/notifications/`. The
   engineer's first pass left it in `TABS`, which would have 404'd —
   **orchestrating session removed it from `TABS`** (same omit-rather-than-
   dead-link rule `ReportsSubNav` already uses for its 4 unbuilt tabs) so
   this ships as 5 working tabs, not 6 with one broken. Add it back the
   moment that page exists. `/console/settings/capacity` deliberately
   excluded as a 7th tab — not in the prototype's `SETUP` array or
   `screen_inventory.md`, same omit-rather-than-invent rule Fix 4 used.

**Verification (Fix 5):** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean on all
touched files; `apps/console` vitest suite 191/191, no regression; dev
server confirmed via curl — all 5 routes return 307 (login redirect), as
expected for an unauthenticated request.

**Reviewed by: redline_reviewer (`86b1941` + `7b3d534`) — clean, no
CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW findings against the change itself.** Confirmed by
direct file-existence check (not grep alone) that all 5 remaining `TABS`
entries resolve to a real `page.tsx`; confirmed zero stale references to
`/console/settings/notifications` anywhere outside the explanatory code
comment; confirmed the `เมนู`→`/console/menu` exact-match routing can't
mis-highlight; confirmed `/console/settings/capacity`'s exclusion is correct
per the prototype/screen_inventory (not present in either). Re-ran
`tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/full vitest suite (191/191) directly — all clean.
**Pre-existing gap surfaced, not introduced by this change, not blocking**:
`/console/settings/capacity` (built in WBS 5.3, commit `90b8b06`) has zero
inbound links from anywhere in the app — orphaned, reachable only by typing
the URL. Flagged for a future decision (add it to the prototype's `SETUP`
array + a tab, or document the intended access path) rather than silently
left unnoticed.

**Incidental fix required to keep WBS 2.2's fidelity gate green:**
`packages/ui/scripts/verify-fidelity.mjs`'s browser bundle broke entirely
(0/21 rows resolving — "process is not defined") once `NavShell` started
importing real `next/link`/`next/navigation` code, which reads
`process.env.*` keys beyond `NODE_ENV` in a plain-browser page with no
Node/webpack `process` global. Added a minimal `process` shim via esbuild's
`banner` option. Re-ran after: back to 18 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 NO_REFERENCE,
matching the pre-fix baseline exactly.

**Also updated:** `.oc-nav`/`.oc-side`/`.oc-subnav` CSS selectors in
`components.css` targeted `button`, now target `a` (both `NavShell` and
`ReportsSubNav` render real `<Link>` anchors, not buttons).
`packages/ui/package.json` gained `next` as an optional peer dependency.

**Verification:** `pnpm --filter @brewledger/ui build`, `npx tsc --noEmit`
(apps/console), `npx eslint` on every touched file, `pnpm lint:boundary`,
the forbidden-copy grep, `packages/ui`'s vitest suite (21/21), and
`verify-fidelity.mjs` all clean/green. `apps/console`'s existing vitest
suite: 184/186 pass — the 2 failures are `DashboardClient.test.tsx`'s stale
2-decimal assertions from Fix 3 above, left for `qa_engineer` to update
rather than edited here. **Not independently confirmed against a live
authenticated render** in this session (`/console` requires phone-OTP login,
not scriptable here) — confirmed instead via `verify-fidelity.mjs`'s real
Playwright/browser render of `NavShell` and via direct code/CSS review;
flagged for `redline_reviewer` to do a live pass.

Implemented by: engineer. **Reviewed by: redline_reviewer — clean, no CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM findings.**
Independently diffed Fix 1's CSS byte-for-byte against `/design/Owner
Console.html`'s real `<style>` block (verbatim match, not paraphrased).
Verified all 5 NavShell route mappings against `docs/design/screen_inventory.md`
and confirmed `reports→pnl` matches the prototype's own default
(`console-reports.js` line 59, `S={screen:'pnl',...}`). Confirmed no other

Function call failed — unrelated to this diff (login is untouched by it).

### Follow-up (2026-08-23) — three report/inventory pages had their own independent width bug, plus MoneyValue's stale 2-decimal default

Found by the user doing a live screenshot comparison of `/console/reports/pnl`
against `/design/Console Reports.html`, after the cross-cutting fix above had
already landed and been signed off. This is a **separate, pre-existing bug**
in the individual pages' own wrapper markup — the cross-cutting fix repaired
the shared shell (`.oc`/`.oc-main`/`NavShell`) but never touched these three
pages' own JSX.

1. **Ad-hoc Tailwind `<main>` wrapper capped width at 672-768px**,
   completely bypassing `.oc-body{max-width:1180px}`, in:
   - `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/reports/pnl/page.tsx` (2 occurrences
     — empty-store branch and normal branch)
   - `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/reports/profit-per-dish/page.tsx`
     (2 occurrences)
   - `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/inventory/page.tsx` (1 occurrence)

   Also invalid HTML: `(app)/layout.tsx` already renders
   `<main className="oc-main">{children}</main>`, so all 5 occurrences were a
   second, nested `<main>` inside that outer one. Fixed by deleting the
   Tailwind `<main>` wrapper in all 5 spots. `PnlClient`/`ProfitPerDishClient`
   already render their own root `<div className="oc-body">`, so their
   normal-branch page.tsx now just returns `<ReportsSubNav/>` + heading +
   the client component as siblings, no extra wrapper needed; their
   empty-store branches keep the `<div className="oc-body">` they already
   had, just unwrapped from `<main>`. `IngredientListClient` (inventory) does
   **not** render its own `.oc-body` (its root is a bare Tailwind
   `flex flex-col gap-4`) so `inventory/page.tsx` now wraps it in
   `<div className="oc-body">` itself.

   Also dropped the ad-hoc Tailwind heading classes
   (`font-serif text-3xl font-bold leading-[1.35] text-ink`) on the pnl and
   profit-per-dish `<h1>`s — `packages/ui/src/components.css` already styles
   bare `h1` (font-thai, weight 800, `--text-6`, `--lh-thai-head`) and
   `IngredientListClient`'s own `<h1>` already relies on that base style with
   no Tailwind override, so the two report pages were the odd ones out. Now
   all three pages defer to the same base rule.

   **Flagged, not fixed (out of this task's scope):** the identical
   ad-hoc-`<main>`-wrapper bug is also present, unchanged, in
   `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/page.tsx` (the dashboard —
   ironically the file this task's own instructions cited as the "correct
   reference pattern," which turned out to be inaccurate — the dashboard's
   `page.tsx` still wraps `{heading}` + `<DashboardClient>`/`<DashboardView>`
   in the same `mx-auto w-full max-w-2xl px-4 py-8` `<main>`, despite the
   prior cross-cutting fix's commit message listing WBS 7.1 as one of the
   screens it fixed), and in at least 8 more pages found by grepping for
   `max-w-2xl|max-w-3xl|mx-auto w-full` across `apps/console`:
   `inventory/[id]/page.tsx`, `orders/[id]/OrderDetailClient.tsx`,
   `settings/subscription/page.tsx`, `settings/store/page.tsx`,
   `settings/payments/page.tsx`, `settings/link/page.tsx`,
   `settings/capacity/page.tsx`, `orders/page.tsx`, `menu/page.tsx`,
   `menu/[id]/page.tsx`. This looks like a repo-wide pattern from before the
   `.oc-body` scaffold existed, not isolated to the three files in scope
   here — recommend a dedicated cross-cutting follow-up pass rather than
   fixing piecemeal per screen.

2. **`MoneyValue` rendered ฿0.00 instead of ฿0 / ฿8,420** on the P&L page
   (confirmed live via the same screenshot) — `PnlClient.tsx` and
   `ProfitPerDishClient.tsx` call `MoneyValue` (4 sites each) with no
   `decimals` prop, defaulting to `MoneyValue`'s own `decimals={2}`. Grepped
   every `MoneyValue`/`PublicMoneyValue` caller in the repo: **zero** ever
   pass `decimals={2}` explicitly — every caller relied on the default, and
   neither prototype helper (`console-reports.js`'s nor `customer-web.js`'s
   own `B()`) ever shows decimals. This confirms the open question the prior
   fix flagged (PROGRESS.md above, Fix 3) was the component's default being
   wrong from WBS 2.2, not a per-caller oversight.

   Fixed by changing `MoneyValue`'s own default from `decimals = 2` to
   `decimals = 0` in `packages/ui/src/components/MoneyValue.tsx`, safe
   project-wide since no caller overrides it. `MetricCard.tsx`'s
   `decimals={0}` kept (now redundant, but left explicit as documentation,
   comment updated). Updated 3 stale `.00`-literal assertions in
   `PnlClient.test.tsx` (`"324.00"→"324"`, `"42.00"→"42"`,
   `"8.00"`/`"196.00"` likewise) — grepped both `packages/ui` and
   `apps/console` for any other `.00`-literal money assertions, found none
   else.

   **Flagged, not fixed (explicitly out of scope):** `PublicMoneyValue.tsx`
   (`apps/shop`'s equivalent component) still defaults to `decimals = 2` and
   was deliberately left untouched. Its own prototype helper
   (`customer-web.js`'s `B()`) also never shows decimals, so the same bug
   likely exists live in Customer Web too — worth a redline_reviewer check
   against a live `apps/shop` render before deciding whether to fix it the
   same way.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean on every touched
file (both `apps/console` and `packages/ui`). RL-2 forbidden-phrase grep
clean. `apps/console`'s full vitest suite: 191/191 passing (was 191 before
this change too — the 3 fixed assertions were failing until updated, not new
tests). Grepped for any other `max-w-2xl`/`max-w-3xl`/`mx-auto w-full` test
assertions that might have gone stale — none found. Dev server (`pnpm dev`,
already running) returns the expected `307` redirect to `/console/login` for
`/console/reports/pnl` — not independently confirmed via an authenticated
live render in this session (no browser/Playwright tooling used here); left
for `redline_reviewer`.

Implemented by: engineer. **Reviewed by: redline_reviewer, together with the follow-up sweep below — see the combined sign-off at the end of this section.**

### Follow-up sweep (2026-08-23) — the remaining 11 files closing out the full 14-file repo-wide fix

Same defect as the 2026-08-23 follow-up above (ad-hoc Tailwind `<main className="mx-auto w-full max-w-{2xl|3xl|xl} px-4 py-8">` wrapper, nested inside `(app)/layout.tsx`'s own `<main className="oc-main">`, bypassing `.oc-body{max-width:1180px}`), fixed in the remaining 11 files that follow-up explicitly flagged but left out of scope:

`(app)/page.tsx` (dashboard), `orders/page.tsx` (2 occurrences — empty-queue and normal branch), `orders/[id]/OrderDetailClient.tsx`, `menu/page.tsx`, `menu/[id]/page.tsx` (2 occurrences — new-item and edit-item branches), `inventory/[id]/page.tsx` (2 occurrences — new-ingredient and edit-ingredient branches), `settings/store/page.tsx`, `settings/payments/page.tsx`, `settings/link/page.tsx`, `settings/subscription/page.tsx`, `settings/capacity/page.tsx`. 15 occurrences total across the 11 files.

`DashboardView` (`(app)/DashboardClient.tsx`) already renders its own root `<div className="oc-body">`, so `(app)/page.tsx` now returns the heading and `DashboardClient`/`DashboardView` as siblings with no extra wrapper — matching the `PnlClient`/`ProfitPerDishClient` pattern from the prior round. Every other child component in this round (`PageEmptyState`, `InboxClient`, `PendingPaymentSection`, `MenuListClient`, `MenuItemEditorForm`, `IngredientEditorForm`, `StoreProfileForm`, `PaymentsSettingsForm`, `StoreLinkQR`, `SubscriptionPlanView`, `CapacitySettingsForm`) does **not** render its own `.oc-body` root (confirmed by reading each one, not assumed), so those 10 files now wrap the child directly in `<div className="oc-body">`, matching `inventory/page.tsx`'s pattern from the prior round. `OrderDetailClient.tsx` (`use client`) got the same treatment at the JSX level — its existing `<header className="oc-top">` already carries the page's own back-button + order-code heading, so the outer `<main>` became a plain `<div className="oc-body">` around the unchanged header + card stack, and the ad-hoc Tailwind classes on its inner `<h1>{order.code}</h1>` (`font-serif text-3xl font-bold leading-[1.35] text-ink`) were dropped in favor of the base `h1` CSS rule, same as the prior round did for the report pages.

Also dropped the same ad-hoc Tailwind heading classes wherever present on a bare page-level `<h1>` (`(app)/page.tsx`, `menu/page.tsx`, `settings/store/page.tsx`, `settings/payments/page.tsx`, `settings/link/page.tsx`, `settings/subscription/page.tsx`, `settings/capacity/page.tsx`, `orders/page.tsx`) — `menu/[id]/page.tsx` and `inventory/[id]/page.tsx` had no page-level `<h1>` of their own (the child form owns its own heading), so nothing to drop there.

This closes the full sweep: 3 files fixed in the original cross-cutting round + 3 in the 2026-08-23 follow-up + these 11 = **14 total files**, all under `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/`.

**Verification:**
```
grep -rln 'className="mx-auto w-full max-w' "apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/"
```
returns zero matches, confirmed by direct re-run after all 11 edits. `grep -rn 'max-w-2xl|max-w-3xl|max-w-xl|mb-6 font-serif text-3xl'` across all of `apps/console` (code and tests) also returns zero — no stale test assertions referencing the removed classes anywhere, so nothing needed updating in the test suite for this round. `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/console) clean. `npx eslint` on all 11 touched files clean. Full `apps/console` vitest suite: **191/191 passing**, same count as before this round (no regression, no test needed touching). `pnpm dev` was already running against local Supabase on port 3000 (not 3001 as initially assumed — confirmed via `Get-NetTCPConnection`); `curl` against `/console`, `/console/orders`, `/console/menu`, `/console/settings/capacity` all return the expected `307` redirect to `/console/login?next=...` (unauthenticated) — not a full authenticated browser render, but confirms none of the 11 edits broke server-side rendering or introduced a runtime error on any of the touched routes.

### Follow-up (2026-08-23) — WBS 4.7 subscription screen rebuilt to the prototype's card design, product-owner override

`docs/design/state_matrix.md`'s original "แผนการใช้งาน" section claimed this
screen "does not exist in the delivered prototype at all" and authored a
feature-comparison-matrix-table layout from that (wrong) premise — WBS 4.7
was built against it. The claim was wrong: `design/console-setup.js` has a
complete `scPlan()` function (line 117, `PLANS` array, registered in
`SCREENS`/`SETUP`) implementing this screen as a 4-card pricing grid — missed
in the `P5 Handoff.md` → `state_matrix.md` extraction, the same class of gap
as GAP-1/GAP-5 (see `docs/design/gaps.md`, now logged as GAP-10 since this
one shipped as a wrong build first, not just missing documentation).

Per **explicit product-owner instruction**, rebuilt `/console/settings/subscription`
(`apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/settings/subscription/{page.tsx,
SubscriptionPlanView.tsx, copy.ts}`) to the prototype's card layout in place
of the feature-matrix table: a current-plan card (name/price/RL-1 fee
sentence/RL-2 always-available sentence), a 4-card `.oc-plans` grid (current
tier shows `ใช้อยู่`, others show an outline `เลือกแพ็กเกจนี้` button, not yet
wired to any upgrade backend — none exists), then an "อัปเกรดแล้วได้อะไรเพิ่ม"
checklist card. All `.oc-plans`/`.oc-plan`/`.oc-planprice`/`.oc-feeline`/
`.oc-rows`/`.oc-row`/`.oc-tick` CSS already existed in
`packages/ui/src/components.css` — no new CSS needed.

**Internal DB enum deliberately left unchanged.** `merchants.subscription_tier`
stays `free | starter | growth | scale`; `packages/shared/src/features.ts`'s
feature-gating logic is untouched. The 4 internal tiers map to the
prototype's 4 card labels **by position only** (display layer):

| Internal tier (unchanged) | Display name | Price | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `free` | ทดลองใช้ | ฿0 | ออเดอร์ 50 รายการ/เดือน · เมนูไม่จำกัด |
| `starter` | เริ่มต้น | ฿199/เดือน | ออเดอร์ไม่จำกัด · รายงานกำไรรายวัน |
| `growth` | ร้านประจำ | ฿449/เดือน | สแกนบิลอัตโนมัติ · กำไรต่อเมนู · คลังวัตถุดิบ |
| `scale` | หลายสาขา | ฿990/เดือน | จัดการหลายสาขา · รายงานรวม · ผู้ใช้หลายคน |

RL-1's fee-disclosure sentence and RL-2's "sale never gated" statement — both
real requirements the withdrawn state-matrix copy correctly captured and
which the prototype's own simpler mockup does not contain — were preserved
verbatim on the rebuilt screen rather than lost in the swap. No renewal date
is shown (the prototype's `ต่ออายุอัตโนมัติ 1 ก.ย. 2569` is a static mock with
no backing column; omitted rather than fabricated, per the same null-over-
guessed-value rule `MoneyValue` follows for cost). No self-serve upgrade

| 3.3 | Render Worker Service Provisioning | 3 | done — verified live on real Render infra against real `brewledger-prod` | 🔴 Manual + ▶️ Claude Code (split entry, medium-risk — orchestrating session closed out directly, no separate redline_reviewer dispatch per updated dispatch policy) | engineer | orchestrating session | (uncommitted) | This row covers the **Claude Code portion only** — the Render account/service creation, dashboard env vars, and the 20-minute-idle cold-start measurement are a manual step still outstanding for the user (WBS 3.3's own manual block, steps 1–8). Built: `worker/src/{index,queue,db,log}.ts`, `worker/src/handlers/{index,types}.ts`, `worker/package.json`, `worker/tsconfig.json`, `render.yaml`, `docs/ops/render.md`; added `worker` to `pnpm-workspace.yaml`. `index.ts` — only route is `GET /healthz` (`{ok, sha, queueDepth, uptimeSec}`), SIGTERM/SIGINT stop the poller, wait for the in-flight job, close the pg pool, exit. `queue.ts` — the exact `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claim query from the WBS prompt via a direct `pg` connection (`DATABASE_URL`), not PostgREST (PostgREST can't express this locking shape). One interpretation call, flagged rather than guessed silently: the WBS prompt's "on failure set status='failed' ... and re-queue ... while attempts < 5" is contradictory as literally written, since the claim query only ever selects `status='pending'` rows — implemented it as status stays/returns to `'pending'` with `run_after` pushed out by exponential backoff (`30s * 2^(attempts-1)`, capped at 3600s) while `attempts < 5`, and only becomes the terminal `'failed'` after the 5th attempt. `db.ts` re-uses WBS 3.2's `createAdminClient` from `packages/shared/src/supabase/admin.ts` via the same direct-subpath pattern the RL-3 eslint zone already targets, plus a `pg.Pool` for the raw claim SQL; top comment states the service_role custody rule per the prompt. `log.ts` — structured JSON (`level/ts/msg` + job context) with a `redact()` helper matching `/key|secret|token|password|authorization/i`, recursive, used on every logged field object. `handlers/index.ts` — registry with all 6 stubs named in the prompt (`ocr_extract`, `cost_recalc`, `daily_aggregate`, `cost_drift_check`, `low_stock_check`, `push_notify`), each logs-and-returns only. **Verified**: `pnpm install`, `pnpm --filter @brewledger/worker build` clean; `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`/`pnpm lint:boundary` all clean repo-wide (7 workspaces now, `worker` included). Real smoke test against a local Docker `supabase start` + `supabase db reset` stack (not skipped): ran the built worker with real `SUPABASE_URL`/`SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`DATABASE_URL` pointed at the local stack, `curl /healthz` returned `{"ok":true,"sha":null,"queueDepth":0,"uptimeSec":...}`, inserted a real `job_queue` row (`push_notify`) via a raw `pg` client, confirmed the poller claimed it on its next tick (`status: pending → done`, `attempts: 1`, `claimed_by` set to the worker's generated id, `processed_at` set) with zero manual intervention — the atomic-claim path, the handler registry dispatch, and the done-marking all proved end to end, not just typechecked. Killed the worker process and ran `supabase stop` afterward; `git status --short -- apps/shop apps/console` confirmed empty both before and after. Not verified (no second concurrent worker instance was run against the same row — SKIP LOCKED's no-double-claim guarantee rests on the well-known Postgres semantics of the query shape itself, not on a race actually exercised here; leaving that as a QA follow-up if wanted). Status `needs review`, not `done` — that's `redline_reviewer`'s call given the RL-3/service_role custody touch. **Post-review fix (orchestrating session):** user's first real Render deploy failed — `Error: Cannot find module '/opt/render/project/src/worker/dist/index.js'` — root cause found from the build log itself: with `Root Directory: worker` set, the Build Command's cwd was actually repo root (`pnpm build` resolved and ran the *root* package's `pnpm -r build` script, confirmed by the log's own `brewledger@0.1.0 build /opt/render/project/src` line) while the Start Command's cwd was `worker/` — a genuine cwd mismatch between build and deploy, not a code defect. Fix: dropped `rootDir` from `render.yaml` entirely, anchored both commands at repo root — `buildCommand: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm --filter "@brewledger/worker..." build` (the `...` is required: it also builds `@brewledger/shared`, which `worker/src/db.ts` imports from `@brewledger/shared/dist/...` — a plain `--filter @brewledger/worker` would have left that import unresolved at runtime, a second latent bug caught during this fix, never yet hit in production only because the first bug masked it) and `startCommand: node worker/dist/index.js`. Verified locally end-to-end: wiped both `worker/dist` and `packages/shared/dist`, ran the exact new build command from repo root, confirmed both dist trees regenerate in correct topological order, then ran `node worker/dist/index.js` from repo root and confirmed it fails on a missing env var (`DATABASE_URL`) rather than a missing module — proving the module-resolution path itself is now correct. Documented the exact required dashboard settings (Root Directory blank, both commands verbatim) in `docs/ops/render.md` since this service was created manually, not via Blueprint, so `render.yaml` isn't auto-enforced — the user must edit the dashboard fields to match. **Second real-deploy round (after dashboard settings fixed):** module resolution now correct, but the poller's first tick logged `{"error":""}` — an empty message on every tick. Root cause: Node's happy-eyeballs dual-stack connect throws `AggregateError` when every attempt fails, and `AggregateError.message` is `""` at the top level (the real reason lives in `.errors`); `queue.ts` was only reading `err.message`. Fixed by adding a `describeError()` helper that unwraps `AggregateError.errors` (`a7af239`) — this is a pure logging/diagnosability fix, not a connectivity fix, but it was necessary to see the real error at all. **Third round:** with the real error visible, found `DATABASE_URL` in the Render dashboard was malformed (`postgresql:postgresql://...`, the scheme duplicated — user had pasted it with an extra prefix) causing pg to mis-parse the username, producing `password authentication failed for user "postgres"` instead of the pooler's expected tenant-suffixed `postgres.<project_ref>`. User corrected the env var by re-copying the pooler string directly from Supabase. **Fourth round:** auth succeeded, connection succeeded, but `relation "job_queue" does not exist` — because `DATABASE_URL` pointed at `brewledger-prod` (`iffvmpeuayvtmfwewgnu`), which had never received any of WBS 3.5/3.6's 22 migrations; every migration to date had only ever been pushed to `brewledger-dev`. Fixed directly: `supabase link --project-ref iffvmpeuayvtmfwewgnu` (temporary), `supabase db push` (all 22 migrations applied cleanly, `seeds: []` — deliberately did not push `seed.sql`'s demo merchant into prod), then `supabase link --project-ref xeoaoabqmqutnsssneiq` to restore the session's normal dev link. **Fifth deploy: clean.** No error-level log lines at all — `worker http server listening`, `poller started`, `Your service is live`, confirmed by the user at `https://brewledger-worker.onrender.com/`. **Still outstanding, both flagged transparently, neither blocking:** (1) the 20-minute-idle cold-start measurement (WBS 3.3's own manual step 8) — user has not yet left the service idle to time it; `docs/ops/render.md`'s cold-start field is still blank. (2) `brewledger-prod`'s DB password was pasted in plaintext into this chat session while debugging the malformed `DATABASE_URL` — flagged immediately as a leaked credential requiring rotation (`docs/security/keys.md`'s own severity bar: full DB access, bypasses RLS entirely, equivalent blast radius to a leaked `service_role` key); user made an explicit, informed call to defer rotation since no real customer data exists in `brewledger-prod` yet and this is pre-launch MVP work — noted here so it isn't silently forgotten before a real launch. |

Real anon-key REST calls (via `supabase projects api-keys`, key not written to any committed file) confirmed all 13 previously-open now-closed tables return `[]` for anon (`ingredients`, `orders`, `order_item_options`, `menu_categories`, `job_queue` spot-checked directly), while the anon-visible tables still filter correctly — `stores` returned only the published `demo-cafe` row, `menu_items` returned only `availability: 'available'` rows. Inserted a real test order + order_item via `service_role` (`order_code = 'RLSTEST1'`), confirmed `public_order_status` and `public_order_lookup` (correct phone+code) both return the exact `{order_code, status, pickup_at, item_name, quantity}` shape with no cost/margin/total field; confirmed `public_order_lookup` returns `[]` for correct-phone+wrong-code and wrong-phone+correct-code; confirmed direct `select` on `orders` as anon still returns `[]` even for the real order (RPC bypass doesn't leak through the table). Deleted both test rows via `service_role` afterward and confirmed zero rows remain (`brewledger-dev` left in its existing seeded state). No deviation from architect's design found — implemented verbatim. `pnpm lint:boundary` clean; `pnpm typecheck` clean except the known pre-existing unrelated `apps/api` → `@brewledger/db` module-resolution failure (not touched by this entry). Did not touch `apps/shop`/`apps/console`. **`qa_engineer` leg (this update)**: wrote `packages/db/tests/rls.test.ts` (51 assertions across all 8 categories of design doc §7 — introspection, anon-zero-rows on the 13 closed tables, anon-filtered-rows on the 5 anon-visible tables including the full-slot-absent and past-slot-absent cases, unpublished-store-invisible-by-exact-slug, cross-tenant isolation looped over all 16 merchant-owned tables for two real fixture merchants, `merchants` self-scoping, `job_queue` no-access for both `anon` and `authenticated`, and the `public_order_status`/`public_order_lookup` RPC contract including the wrong-phone/wrong-code/direct-table-still-closed cases), plus two new test helpers: `tests/helpers/supabase-clients.ts` (real `anon`/`service_role` clients, and a JWT locally minted with the well-known local GoTrue `JWT_SECRET` to produce a real `authenticated`-role session per fixture merchant — necessary, not just convenient, because this stack's `supabase/config.toml` has phone-OTP-only auth with no local SMS provider configured, so `supabase start` itself prints "Disabling phone login" and a real `signInWithOtp` session isn't obtainable locally at all) and `tests/helpers/rls-fixture.ts` (`createFullStoreFixture` — one merchant with a row in all 16 merchant-owned tables + `job_queue`; `createVisibilityFixture` — published/unpublished store pair with hidden/visible menu items and open/full/past pickup slots). **Fixtures are committed, not `withRollback`-wrapped** (unlike WBS 3.5's tests): every assertion here goes through the local PostgREST HTTP API via `@supabase/supabase-js`, which opens its own Postgres connection per request — under READ COMMITTED isolation a still-open transaction on a separate `pg` connection is invisible to that request, so `withRollback`'s "insert, assert, always roll back" mechanism cannot produce visible rows for the anon/authenticated/service_role HTTP clients at all. Each fixture instead returns an explicit `cleanup()` (deletes the fixture's `auth.users` row, which cascades merchants → stores → every store-scoped table) called from `afterAll`. **A genuine, blocking environment gap was found, not just a missing test**: on a fresh `supabase start` + `supabase db reset` of the LOCAL Docker stack (CLI 2.115.0), no role — `anon`, `authenticated`, **or `service_role`** — has any `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` grant on any `public` schema table; every direct PostgREST table query for every role returns `42501 permission denied for table ...` (confirmed directly via `curl` and via `information_schema.role_table_grants`, which shows only `TRUNCATE`/`REFERENCES`/`TRIGGER` granted to `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` — never `SELECT`). RLS itself is correctly enabled and the policies are correctly defined (`pg_policies` shows the right policies with the right predicates, confirmed independently of the anon-key path via a raw `postgres`-role query) — the gap is one layer up, at the ordinary Postgres `GRANT`, which PostgREST checks before RLS ever evaluates. Root cause, by elimination: `supabase/config.toml`'s `[api] auto_expose_new_tables` is commented out, and this CLI version's own comment states "When unset, new entities are NOT auto-exposed, matching the new cloud default" — migration `0021_rls.sql` never issues an explicit `grant select/insert/update/delete ... to anon, authenticated` (only the two RPCs get an explicit `grant execute`), so nothing in the migration chain ever grants table access to any of the three API-facing roles locally. This most likely explains why the `engineer` leg's live-`brewledger-dev` verification looked clean (hosted projects are provisioned with these grants by Supabase's own project-init bootstrap, independent of this CLI-local toggle) while the local Docker loop — the one this whole test suite, and every future WBS entry's automated local testing, depends on — is currently unable to reach ANY table through the API layer at all, for any role. Effect on THIS run: 9 of 51 assertions passed (§7.1 introspection, run via the raw `pg`/`postgres`-role connection which bypasses PostgREST grants entirely, and §7.8's RPC tests, which work because the RPCs carry their own explicit `grant execute`) — the other 42 (§7.2 anon-zero-rows in the wrong way — the `service_role` cross-check itself fails, which is a stronger failure than a masked pass; §7.3, §7.5, §7.6, most of §7.7) fail on `42501`, not on a real RLS filtering defect. **Did not patch this myself** (`supabase/config.toml` or a grant-adding migration is `engineer`/`architect` territory, not QA's) — flagging clearly for `engineer` to add explicit `grant select, insert, update, delete on <table> to authenticated;` / `grant select on <table> to anon;` per the design's own table list (or set `auto_expose_new_tables = true` and confirm it actually back-fills grants on a `db reset`, not just future tables) in a follow-up to `0021_rls.sql`, after which this exact suite should be rerun with zero code changes — it is written to the correct target contract already. `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint:boundary` clean; `schema.test.ts` (WBS 3.5, re-run as a regression check) still 17/17 green on the same freshly-reset local stack, confirming the reset itself and the pre-existing suite are unaffected. Did not touch `apps/shop`/`apps/console`. Status intentionally left `needs review`, not `done` — that setting is `redline_reviewer`'s alone. **Orchestrating session (`d169240`)**: fixed the grant gap directly rather than re-dispatching — added a "SECTION 0: PostgREST role grants" to `0021_rls.sql` (`grant usage on schema public` + `grant all on all tables/sequences`/`grant execute on all functions` to `anon, authenticated, service_role`, plus matching `alter default privileges` for future tables), mirrored into `supabase/migrations/0021_rls.sql`. Safe because RLS (SECTION 1 onward) is deny-by-default per table regardless of the grant — a grant only permits the attempt, RLS decides the rows. Verified by re-running `supabase start` + `db reset --local` on a fresh stack and the full test suite: **59 passed, 1 skipped (opt-in reset test), 0 failed** — all 42 previously-42501-failing assertions now pass with zero test-file changes, confirming qa_engineer's suite was already written to the correct contract. Separately verified against the live `brewledger-dev` project via `information_schema.role_table_grants` that it already has full grants for all three roles from Supabase's own hosted bootstrap (confirmed directly, not just inferred) — so this fix is local-Docker-parity only; `brewledger-dev` needed no change, and since Supabase tracks applied migrations by version number rather than content hash, `0021`'s remote status is unaffected by this local content edit. Stopped the local stack afterward (`supabase stop`). Status left at `needs review` — next: `redline_reviewer`. **`redline_reviewer` leg (this update, fully independent, re-derived everything, took nothing on the prior legs' account on faith):** (1) `docs/db/rls_design.md` vs. `packages/db/migrations/0021_rls.sql` — read both top to bottom; the implementation is verbatim against the design (all 18 `enable row level security` statements, `auth_store_ids()` signature byte-identical, all 14 direct-`store_id` merchant policies + 2 one-join policies + `merchants` self-scoping policy, the 5 anon SELECT predicates, both RPCs) — confirmed `diff -q packages/db/migrations/0021_rls.sql supabase/migrations/0021_rls.sql` is empty (byte-identical mirror). §2.1 (`menu_categories` no anon policy, deferred to WBS 3.7's serializer) and §2.2 (`job_queue` zero policies for both roles, since `payload`/`last_error` may carry unvetted internal detail and a merchant policy would need a narrow future RPC instead) — read both, reasoning still holds independently: neither table's public/merchant-only need is well served by a blanket table grant, and nothing in the schema has changed since the design was written that would undercut either call. (2) Read all 5 anon SELECT predicates directly in the SQL — column names (`is_published`, `availability`, `store_id`, `menu_item_id`, `option_group_id`, `is_open`, `booked_count`, `capacity`, `slot_start`) all verified against the real migrations; logic matches published-only / non-hidden / open-not-full-future exactly. Grepped `0021_rls.sql` for `to anon` myself — exactly 7 hits: the 5 SELECT policies plus the 2 RPC `grant execute` statements; zero anon policy anywhere else in the file. (3) Confirmed via `pg_policies` on a freshly-reset local stack that `orders`/`order_items` carry exactly one policy each (`merchant_rw_orders`/`merchant_rw_order_items`, both `{authenticated}` only, `ALL`) — no anon entry for either. Confirmed via `pg_proc`/`pg_namespace` that `public_order_status`, `public_order_lookup`, and `auth_store_ids` are all `prosecdef = t` (security definer) with `proconfig = {search_path=public}`. Read both RPC return signatures directly — `{order_code, status, pickup_at, item_name, quantity}`, no field matching `/cost|margin|profit|fee|expense|stock/`. Read `public_order_lookup`'s `where` clause — `o.order_code = p_order_code and o.customer_phone = p_phone`, a genuine `AND`, not an `OR`, both params required with no default. (4) SECTION 0's grant fix — read it directly; reasoned through it independently and then proved it empirically rather than trusting the reasoning alone: started a fresh local stack (`supabase start`, `supabase db reset --local`, all 22 migrations incl. `0021` applied cleanly), then ran manual adversarial HTTP calls as `anon` beyond what the automated suite covers — `INSERT` into `job_queue` (zero policies at all) correctly 401/`42501`s; `INSERT` into `orders` (RPC-only design) correctly 401/`42501`s; **`UPDATE` on `menu_categories`** (merchant-only, no anon policy) and **`DELETE` on `ingredients`** (cost data, no anon policy) both returned HTTP 200 with `Prefer: return=representation` showing `[]` — zero rows matched, not "permission denied" but genuinely zero visible rows under RLS's `USING` clause — then independently re-queried both tables via `service_role` and confirmed the seed data (`ingredients`: Milk/Coffee Beans/Sugar; `menu_categories`: "Coffee") was completely unchanged after the attempts. This confirms `GRANT ALL` combined with deny-by-default RLS is genuinely equivalent to a narrower `GRANT SELECT` for these zero-policy/merchant-only tables — the broad grant permits the attempt, RLS's row filter (empty set, since no policy matches) makes every write a no-op regardless of operation type. Also independently minted a JWT for a random `auth.uid()` with no corresponding `merchants` row and confirmed `select * from stores` returns `[]` (not an error, not the full table) — `auth_store_ids()` correctly resolves to nothing for an unmatched uid. (5) Ran the real suite myself on the freshly-reset stack: `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db test` → **59 passed, 1 skipped (the opt-in migration-from-scratch reset test), 0 failed** — matches the claimed count exactly, not taken on faith. (6) Read `packages/db/tests/helpers/rls-fixture.ts` and `helpers/supabase-clients.ts` directly — fixtures use a real autocommit `pg` client (`postgres` superuser) to insert real rows into `auth.users`/`merchants`/`stores`/etc., not application-layer mocks; `mintAuthenticatedJwt` signs a real HS256 JWT with the exact `JWT_SECRET` (`super-secret-jwt-token-with-at-least-32-characters-long`) that `supabase start` printed in this session — confirmed the printed value matches the hardcoded fallback in `supabase-clients.ts` character-for-character. (7) `docs/security/rls.md` — read in full, cross-checked its policy table row by row against the actual `pg_policies` output and the SQL predicates in `0021_rls.sql`; every row matches, including the `job_queue`/`menu_categories` "no anon policy, here's why" rows and the RPC grant-execute-to-anon-only note. No inaccuracy found. (8) `git diff faa5e47..978157f --stat -- apps/shop apps/console` — empty, confirmed myself, zero touch to either live-on-Vercel app across the entire WBS 3.6 commit range. (9) `git log -p faa5e47..978157f`, added lines only (`^+`), grepped for the real `xeoaoabqmqutnsssneiq` project ref and `eyJhbGci` JWT prefixes — the only two `eyJhbGci` values present are the well-known local-CLI demo anon/service_role keys (verified character-for-character against what `supabase start` printed in this session); zero occurrences of the real project ref. Ran `scripts/test-import-boundary-regression.sh` (WBS 3.2's own regression check) — still passes. `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:boundary`, `pnpm lint:functions`, `pnpm build` all clean; the previously-known `apps/api` typecheck failure is simply gone. `grep` for the RL-2 nag phrases in `apps/console/` — zero hits (no console changes made). The `apps/shop/.next/static/` forbidden-string grep from `engineer.md`'s own checklist returns hits for `margin` — verified by hand these are 100% CSS `margin`/`marginTop`/`marginBottom` properties from React/Next.js framework code, not a data leak; apps/shop was never touched (`git status --short -- apps/shop apps/console` confirmed empty at both the start and end of this leg, and again immediately after two separate scratch-file plant/remove drills used to prove the anti-spread and boundary rules against real `.tsx`/`.ts` files). `docker` stack stopped (`supabase stop`) after all verification. Not implemented, per design: `PublicPaymentIntent` (deferred to WBS 5.5), any `console-*` business-logic function (none designed yet). **`qa_engineer` leg (this update, `af1e0da`):** implemented all 6 items of §7's test contract, all run and passing against a real local `supabase start` + `supabase db reset` + `supabase functions serve --no-verify-jwt --import-map supabase/functions/deno.json` stack (not mocked — the whole point, same discipline as WBS 3.5/3.6). New workspace package `supabase/functions/_tests` (added to `pnpm-workspace.yaml`) since these are Node/vitest tests of the Deno Edge Functions HTTP surface, not a servable function themselves. **Engineering call on snapshot-test approach**: HTTP-against-a-running-`supabase functions serve`, not a direct TS import of the handler modules — the handlers are Deno-only code (`Deno.serve`, `npm:` specifiers, `Deno.env.get`) that cannot run in a Node/vitest process without a shim layer that would itself go untested; hitting the real served function over real HTTP proves the real Deno runtime + real import map + real handler code cooperate to produce the exact response, the same "a mock proves nothing about RLS/triggers" principle applied one layer up to the Edge Function runtime. (1) **Response snapshots** — `supabase/functions/_tests/public_snapshots.test.ts` + `__snapshots__/public-{store,menu,slots,order-status}.json`, one per endpoint, seeded from `packages/db/seed.sql`'s `demo-cafe` fixture. UUIDs and ISO timestamps are normalized to `<uuid>`/`<timestamptz>` placeholders on both sides before comparison (`helpers/clients.ts`'s `normalizeForSnapshot`) since `gen_random_uuid()` ids and `now()`-relative timestamps can never be pinned to a literal value — every other field is compared exactly, so an added/removed/renamed DTO field fails the diff. `public-slots` needed a dynamically-inserted, service_role-created future slot (capacity 5/booked 2 → `remaining: 3`) because the seed's own same-day 07:00–10:00 slots are always past `now()` by test time — RLS's own `anon_read_open_pickup_slots` predicate excludes them, same gap the engineer leg already hit and worked around manually; this test automates that workaround with `beforeAll`/`afterAll` insert-then-delete via `service_role`. `public-order-status` similarly inserts and cleans up one real order+item (no `pickup_slot_id`, keeping `pickupAt` deterministically `null`). Also asserts the 404/400 error paths, the code+correct-phone vs. code+wrong-phone `AND` (not `OR`) semantics, and that every item's `categoryId` genuinely resolves against the returned `categories` array pre-normalization. (2) **Forbidden-field CI scan** — `scripts/scan-forbidden-fields.mjs` (also wired as `pnpm scan:forbidden-fields`), exporting `loadForbiddenSubstrings`/`scanForForbiddenFields` so `forbidden_field_scan.test.ts` can both assert the real committed snapshots are clean against the real `docs/design/forbidden_fields.json` AND prove the scanner actually detects a hit (plants a temp fixture file with `cost_satang` as a JSON key and a lowercase `margin` substring embedded mid-value in a scratch temp dir, asserts both are caught, cleans up). (3) **Zod strict-mode extra-key tests** — `packages/shared/src/serializers/public.schema.test.ts`, one `describe` per schema (7 schemas: store, menu category, menu item, option group, option, slot, order status), each with a "valid DTO accepted" sanity case plus one-or-more "rejects with an extra key like `costSatang`/`marginSatang`/`capacity`/`bookedCount`/`paymentConfirmedBy`/`totalCostSnapshotSatang`" cases — 17 tests total, all against the real `.strict()` schemas, not a re-derived copy. (4) **Auth guard 401 test** — `supabase/functions/_tests/console_auth_guard.test.ts` against a new synthetic handler `supabase/functions/qa-console-auth-probe/index.ts` (zero business logic, wraps `withConsoleAuth`, echoes only `ctx.merchantId` on success), per the design's own "no real console-* function exists yet... a synthetic test handler... in the interim" allowance. Named `qa-console-auth-probe`, not `_test-console-auth-probe` as originally drafted — confirmed empirically that the Supabase CLI treats any leading-underscore directory under `supabase/functions/` as shared code (same convention as `_shared/`) and silently never serves it; also avoided a `console-*` prefix so this scaffold isn't mistakenly swept into the `console-*` import-boundary zone's coverage claims. Covers: no `Authorization` header → 401 empty body; malformed bearer → 401 empty body; well-formed-but-wrong-signature JWT → 401 empty body (proves signature verification, not just decoding); expired JWT → 401 empty body; a combined no-leak assertion across all three negative cases (`bodyText === ""` plus a regex guard against stack-trace/`Error:`/`Deno.` substrings); and a positive-path sanity case (valid JWT → 200 with exactly `{ok, merchantId}`, nothing else) so the negative-path passes aren't just the guard failing closed unconditionally. **A genuine environment gap was found, not a WBS 3.7 code bug**: `verifyConsoleRequest` calls `supabase.auth.getUser()`, which round-trips to the real GoTrue `/auth/v1/user` endpoint (unlike PostgREST, which only checks the JWT signature/claims and never queries `auth.users`) — GoTrue's Go struct scans `confirmation_token`/`recovery_token`/`email_change_token_new` as non-nullable strings, and `packages/db/seed.sql`'s `insert into auth.users` for the demo merchant never sets them, so they default to `NULL` and GoTrue's own `/user` endpoint 500s (`"converting NULL to string is unsupported"`, confirmed directly against the `supabase_auth_brewLedger` container logs) for a valid, unexpired, correctly-signed JWT referencing that seeded user. This does **not** affect any of the negative-path 401 assertions above — GoTrue rejects a malformed/wrongly-signed/expired token at the verification step, before the row scan ever runs (confirmed in the same logs: those requests fail with `bad_jwt`, never reaching SQL) — but it silently blocks the positive path for anyone reusing the seeded demo merchant. Worked around inside this test only, without touching `packages/db/seed.sql`: `helpers/auth-user-fixture.ts`'s `createWellFormedAuthUserFixture()` inserts (via a raw `pg` client, cleaned up in `afterAll`) a dedicated `auth.users` + `merchants` row with those three token columns explicitly set to `''` (matching how `phone_change_token`/`email_change_token_current`/`reauthentication_token` already default to `''` elsewhere in the same table). **Flagging for `engineer`/`architect`, not fixing**: `packages/db/seed.sql`'s demo merchant cannot successfully authenticate through any code path that calls GoTrue's `/user` endpoint (i.e., any real `console-*` function built on `withConsoleAuth` as designed, tested against the demo merchant specifically) until the seed insert sets `confirmation_token = ''`, `recovery_token = ''`, `email_change_token_new = ''` explicitly. (5) **Lint rule proof-of-concept CI step** — `scripts/test-lint-no-db-row-spread-regression.sh` (`pnpm test:lint-no-db-row-spread`), mirroring `scripts/test-import-boundary-regression.sh`'s plant/assert/remove shape: copies `eslint-rules/__fixtures__/no-db-row-spread.violation.ts.disabled` into `supabase/functions/public-store/`, runs `pnpm lint:functions`, asserts the exact message `"RL-3 violation: build public DTOs field by field. See WBS 3.7."` appears **twice** (once per violation case in the fixture — the imported-`Row`-type case and the local `/Row$/`-suffix-alias case — a stronger check than "the message appeared at all", since it would have missed a regression where only one of the two cases still fired), removes the copy, confirms clean again. Ran it for real: PASS. (6) **Cross-import boundary test** — `scripts/test-functions-import-boundary-regression.sh` (`pnpm test:functions-import-boundary`), same shape, both directions, deliberately planting scratch files **nested two directories deep** (`public-store/deep/nested/...`, `console-orders/deep/nested/...`) rather than directly inside the function directory — this is the exact shape of the minimatch bug the engineer leg already found and fixed (`"./supabase/functions/public-*"` bare glob never matched nested files; fixed to `"./supabase/functions/public-*/**"`), so this script would have caught a regression back to the bare-glob form that the original shallow-only manual check would have missed. Ran it for real: PASS, both directions, both cleared afterward. **Verification, all done directly**: `pnpm --filter @brewledger/functions-tests test` (20/20 pass), `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shared test` (21/21 pass, after clearing a stale gitignored `dist/` directory left over from a prior build that was shadowing the real `.ts` test with a broken pre-built `.js` copy — a local environment artifact, not a repo defect, `dist/` is `.gitignore`d), `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db test` (59 passed, 1 skipped — unchanged regression baseline, confirming this leg didn't disturb WBS 3.5/3.6's suite), both regression scripts (PASS), `node scripts/scan-forbidden-fields.mjs` (clean, 0 hits against 21 forbidden substrings), `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:boundary`, `pnpm lint:functions` all clean. Did not touch `apps/shop`/`apps/console` (`git status --short -- apps/shop apps/console` empty throughout). Stopped the local Docker stack after all verification. **Closeout (orchestrating session, per explicit user request to cut redundant re-verification on already well-scrutinized entries):** fixed the qa_engineer-found `seed.sql` bug directly (`33af146` — `confirmation_token`/`recovery_token`/`email_change_token_new` set to `''` on the demo merchant's `auth.users` insert). Skipped a full independent `redline_reviewer` dispatch — three prior legs (architect design, engineer with 2 self-caught/fixed real bugs, qa_engineer with 1 self-caught/flagged real bug, all independently verified against real local infra, none of it self-certified) is already substantial scrutiny for a second-line-of-defence entry. Did a condensed check instead: `git diff --stat d7ec580..HEAD -- apps/shop apps/console` empty, `pnpm lint:boundary`/`pnpm lint:functions` both clean. Marking `done` on that basis. |

| 3.8 | Supabase Storage for Bill Images | 3 | done | Manual (bucket creation) + engineer | engineer | redline_reviewer | (uncommitted) | RL-3 touch: a supplier invoice reveals purchase prices, exactly what RL-3 protects — `bills` is never public. `packages/db/migrations/0022_storage_policies.sql`, mirrored byte-identical into `supabase/migrations/0022_storage_policies.sql` (`diff -q` confirmed): 8 policies on `storage.objects` — `bills` (INSERT/SELECT/DELETE to `authenticated`, predicate `(storage.foldername(name))[1]::uuid in (select auth_store_ids())`, zero anon policy of any kind) and `menu-images` (SELECT to `anon`, INSERT/SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE to `authenticated` with the same store-scoped predicate). Path convention `{store_id}/{file}.ext` per bucket — `storage.foldername(name)[1]` is the first path segment within the object name, not a literal `bills/` prefix (the `bucket_id` column already scopes by bucket). **Local Docker bucket availability**: the manual dashboard step (WBS 3.8's Thai instructions) only applies to `brewledger-dev`/`brewledger-prod`, which this session never touched. For local Docker, added a declarative `[storage.buckets.bills]` / `[storage.buckets."menu-images"]` block to `supabase/config.toml` (same names/public-private split/1MiB limit/allowed MIME types as the manual instructions) — confirmed `supabase start` + `supabase db reset` provisions both automatically ("Creating Storage bucket: bills" / "menu-images" in the reset log), so the local stack was NOT missing the buckets during any of this work; `storage.test.ts`'s `beforeAll` also has a service-role `createBucket` fallback in case a future stale cached snapshot predates this config, so the suite degrades to self-healing rather than silently skipping real assertions. `packages/shared/src/storage/compress.ts` — `compressImage(file, opts?)`, Canvas API only, no new runtime dependency. Longest-edge 1600px default, JPEG quality steps down from 0.8 in 0.05 increments to a floor of 0.5 until ≤200KB (quota comment: 1GB free tier ÷ 4MB raw ≈ 250 bills vs ÷200KB ≈ 5,000). EXIF orientation read by hand-parsing the JPEG APP1/TIFF/IFD0 bytes (tag 0x0112) and corrected via the canonical 8-case `ctx.transform(...)` matrix before drawing. The DOM-dependent glue (`Image`/`document`/`canvas.toBlob`) is isolated from the pure, testable pieces (`readExifOrientation`, `computeScaledDimensions`, `computeOrientedCanvasSize`, `applyOrientationTransform`, `runCompressionLoop`) — see that split's rationale below. `packages/shared/src/storage/bills.ts` — `billObjectPath`, `uploadBill(client, file, storeId, invoiceId)`, `getBillSignedUrl(client, path)` (300s expiry constant); never logs/returns a raw public URL. One signature divergence from the WBS prompt's literal text: both functions take a `SupabaseClient` as their first argument (the prompt's signature omits it) — necessary, since the client must be the merchant's own `authenticated` session for the RLS policies above to actually apply; a service_role client would silently bypass the check these functions exist to go through. `packages/shared/src/storage/usage.ts` — `getBucketUsage`/`getAllBucketUsage(admin, buckets?)`, sums `storage.objects.metadata->size` per bucket via `.schema("storage")`; requires the service_role admin client (ops-level aggregate across all stores, not merchant-scoped) — reporting only, not wired into a monitor (that's WBS 3.10, per the dictionary's own scope). Added `"lib": ["ES2022", "DOM"]` to `packages/shared/tsconfig.json` (this package already ships into both browser bundles; `compress.ts` is the first file in it to reference DOM-only globals). **Verified against a real local `supabase start` + `db reset` stack, not assumed**: queried `pg_policies`/`pg_class.relrowsecurity`/`storage.buckets` directly via a real `pg` connection — all 8 policies present with the right `roles`/`cmd`, RLS enabled, `bills` `public=false`/`menu-images` `public=true`, both `file_size_limit=1048576` and `allowed_mime_types` matching the manual instructions exactly. Wrote and ran `packages/shared/tests/storage/storage.test.ts` (13 tests, real HTTP against local PostgREST/Storage, same `pg`-fixture + minted-JWT discipline as `packages/db/tests/rls.test.ts`) — real merchant-A/merchant-B store fixtures, real uploaded objects: anon cannot download/list/sign-URL/upload a `bills` object; merchant B cannot delete merchant A's bill (object still present afterward, verified via `service_role`); **merchant A cannot create a signed URL for merchant B's bill path, and the reverse — both asserted directly**, the core WBS 3.8 acceptance criterion; the owning merchant's own signed URL resolves with a real `fetch` (200); the signed URL's embedded JWT `token` query param decodes to `exp - iat ≤ 300` (not just "we passed 300 to the SDK" — the actual returned token's expiry is measured); `menu-images` anon download succeeds (public by design) while anon upload still fails. One real bug caught by this suite during writing, not left in: the `menu-images` upload test initially failed with `415 InvalidMimeType` for a bare `Uint8Array` upload — the bucket's `allowed_mime_types` restriction was rejecting the default `text/plain` content-type, a genuine confirmation the MIME allow-list from the manual instructions is actually enforced, not a test bug to shrug off; fixed by passing `contentType: "image/webp"` explicitly. **Adaptation, not silent skip**: `compressImage()` itself was NOT integration-tested end-to-end — it calls `Image`/`document`/`URL.createObjectURL`/`canvas.toBlob`, real browser-only globals. Attempted `canvas@3.2.3` as a devDependency to get real pixel rendering in Node: it resolves and installs via pnpm, but ships no prebuilt binary for this Node/OS combination and there is no native toolchain here to build one from source (consistent with this repo's existing precedent of keeping native image libraries out — `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `ignoredBuiltDependencies: sharp`); removed the dependency again after confirming the failure (`git diff --stat` on `package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` clean before/after). Wrote `packages/shared/src/storage/compress.test.ts` (23 tests) against every DOM-free piece instead: real hand-built JPEG/EXIF byte fixtures (little- and big-endian TIFF, orientations 1/3/6/8) for `readExifOrientation`; the "comes out upright" contract for `computeOrientedCanvasSize` — a landscape 1600×1200 source under orientation 6 (the common sideways-phone case) must produce a portrait 1200×1600 canvas, not a stretched one; the canonical transform matrix per orientation via a recording fake `ctx`; and `runCompressionLoop` against a synthetic-but-size-vs-quality-realistic encoder (quadratic in quality) proving the loop both stops early when under target and never asks for a quality below the 0.5 floor, stepping in exact 0.05 increments. Found and fixed one real off-by-4-bytes bug in the test's own JPEG fixture builder while getting these green (missing `o += 4` after the final TIFF write) — not a bug in `compress.ts` itself, but flagged rather than silently patched around. Added `pg`/`@types/pg` as devDependencies to `@brewledger/shared` (mirroring `@brewledger/db`'s existing pattern) for the real-fixture tests. **Full verification**: `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shared test` → 57 passed (23 compress + 13 storage RLS + 21 pre-existing serializer/admin-guard, scoped to `src`+`tests` — the bare `vitest run` glob also picks up a stale gitignored `dist/*.test.js` from a prior build, the same known non-issue qa_engineer already documented in the WBS 3.7 row, not a WBS 3.8 regression); `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shared typecheck` clean; `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db test` → 59 passed, 1 skipped (unchanged baseline, confirming 0022 doesn't disturb 0021's RLS suite); `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db typecheck` clean; `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`/`pnpm lint:boundary` all clean repo-wide. Did not touch `apps/shop`/`apps/console` (`git status --short -- apps/shop apps/console` empty before and after). Stopped the local stack after verification. **`redline_reviewer` leg (this update, fully independent, no memory of writing any of it, re-derived rather than trusted):** `diff` on the migration mirror pair — byte-identical. Read `0022_storage_policies.sql` directly: `(storage.foldername(name))[1]::uuid in (select auth_store_ids())` is correct Postgres/Storage syntax — `storage.foldername()` is Supabase's built-in helper splitting the object `name` on `/` and returning all-but-the-last segment as `text[]`, so `[1]` is genuinely the first path segment (the store-id folder), and casting it to `uuid` before the `in` against `auth_store_ids()` (itself `uuid[]`-returning per `0021_rls.sql`) is the right shape; confirmed zero `to anon` policy anywhere in the `bills` section (grepped the file myself) and exactly one `to anon` policy on `menu-images` (`anon_select_menu_images`, `for select` only — no accidental anon write policy). | 3.10 | Free-Tier Survival Kit — Keep-alive, Backup and Quota Monitoring | 3 | done (self-certified — see note) | Manual (GitHub secrets, restore drill, threshold test) + engineer | engineer | engineer (self-verified, no separate reviewing leg) | (uncommitted) | **Process note:** this entry has `Red Line Touch: None` per the dictionary, and the dispatching instruction explicitly directed the engineer leg to self-verify and mark `done` directly rather than route through `redline_reviewer`/`qa_engineer`, citing "this project's current policy of skipping redundant review chains for non-red-line-touching entries." That directive is **not** written anywhere in `.claude/agents/WORKFLOW.md` or this file's own stated update rule ("Never self-certify") as of this commit — flagging that explicitly rather than quietly complying, so a human can confirm or correct the policy. Built: `.github/workflows/keepalive.yml` (6-hourly Supabase anon-REST + Render `/healthz` ping, plus a dedicated 23:00 UTC/06:00 Bangkok Render-only morning-warmup job), `.github/workflows/backup.yml` (nightly 18:00 UTC `pg_dump --no-owner` + gzip + per-table manifest, both uploaded as 90-day artifacts, alerts on failure), `scripts/check-quota.mjs` (DB size + per-bucket storage bytes + largest-table row counts vs. 500MB/1GB Supabase Free limits, 70%/85% thresholds overridable via env, 7-day growth projection persisted via a JSON history file, posts to `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` every run, non-zero exit at alert threshold), `.github/workflows/quota.yml` (daily cron + `workflow_dispatch` threshold-override inputs for the mandated alert test, `actions/cache` persists `quota-history.json` across runs), `scripts/restore-drill.mjs` (gunzip → `psql` restore → per-table row-count diff against the backup manifest → PASS/FAIL table), `/docs/ops/free_tier.md` (full limits table, Supabase/Render/Vercel numbers WebFetched live against vendor pages on 2026-08-19 and matched the dictionary exactly; Float16's pricing page 404'd, so that row is carried from the dictionary and marked not independently re-verified; empty Restore drill log section with a bold "not a backup until restored" statement). Root `package.json` gained a `pg` devDependency (query the DB directly via `SUPABASE_DB_URL`, no Storage-HTTP-API round trip needed since `storage.objects` lives in the same Postgres instance) and two convenience scripts (`ops:check-quota`, `ops:restore-drill`); these are plain `.mjs` scripts outside the `pnpm -r typecheck`/`pnpm -r lint` workspace loop, same convention as the pre-existing `scripts/scan-forbidden-fields.mjs`. **Verified for real, not just written:** ran a local Docker `supabase start` stack throughout. `check-quota.mjs` executed against it — real DB-size/storage/row-count query, real webhook POST to a local mock HTTP server, real threshold-lowering triggering `ALERT` severity and exit code 1, real 7-day growth-rate projection math cross-checked by hand (seeded a synthetic 3-day-old history entry, confirmed the projected days-remaining figure). `restore-drill.mjs`'s `loadManifest`/`formatResultsTable` exercised directly; its `countRows`-equivalent query proven against the live local DB. Found and fixed a real bug this way: `pg_dump`'s default output (and separately, `--clean`) both fail outright when replayed onto a fresh Supabase-provisioned scratch database — plain dump hits `schema "public" already exists`, `--clean` hits `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS ... ON public.stores` erroring on a relation that was never created in that target (`IF EXISTS` suppresses "policy not found", not "relation not found"). Fixed by dropping `--clean`/`ON_ERROR_STOP` from the restore path entirely and doing a full real end-to-end proof instead: `docker exec`'d a real `pg_dump --no-owner` from the local stack, gzipped it, created a genuine second scratch database in the same Postgres instance, replayed the dump into it with plain `psql` (tolerating the handful of expected "already exists"/"permission denied to change default privileges" preamble errors), then queried real row counts on both source and target — **exact match** across all six manifest tables (`orders`, `order_items`, `payments`, `stock_ledger`, `purchase_invoices`, `daily_financials`), scratch database dropped immediately after. This proves the dump→restore→row-count-match mechanism end-to-end for real, but is explicitly **not** the mandated pilot restore drill (WBS 3.10 acceptance criterion) — that requires a dump artifact from a real `backup.yml` run against `brewledger-prod` (needs `SUPABASE_DB_URL` secret, not yet set) restored into a true scratch Supabase project or fresh local stack by a human, logged in `/docs/ops/free_tier.md`'s Restore drill log, which remains empty. Also could not exercise `restore-drill.mjs`'s own `psql` subprocess invocation end-to-end on this Windows dev machine (no native `psql`/`pg_dump` binary on `PATH`, and Node's `spawnSync` without `shell:true` does not resolve extension-less commands or `.cmd` shims the way a shell would on Windows) — not a script defect, GitHub Actions' `ubuntu-latest` runner has a real `psql` binary and this path is exactly what `pg_dump`/`psql` were both installed for in `backup.yml`; still, `restore-drill.mjs`'s subprocess-invocation line itself is unexercised in this session, only its logic functions. `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:boundary` all clean repo-wide. `grep` for the five forbidden console phrases against `apps/console/` — empty (this entry never touches `apps/console`). `git status --short -- apps/shop apps/console` — empty before and after. | 4.1 | Phone OTP Authentication | 4 | done | Manual (SMS provider) + engineer, then qa_engineer, then redline_reviewer (RL-3 disjoint-auth touch) | engineer | redline_reviewer | (uncommitted at time of writing) | Manual step closed 2026-08-19 — Vonage selected after Twilio (card wall) and MessageBird (dashboard unreachable) both failed; live end-to-end OTP delivery to a real Thai number confirmed via `curl` against `/auth/v1/otp`. Email provider disabled, SMS rate limit set to 5/hour (mirrored in `supabase/config.toml`), OTP expiry 600s. Sender ID falls back to Vonage's shared "NXSMS" for Thai numbers (carrier-side, not a defect) — brand identity carried in the SMS body template instead (`supabase/config.toml`'s `[auth.sms].template`). Full account/vendor/cost history in `docs/ops/auth.md`. **This dispatch found substantial uncommitted WIP already in the tree from an earlier, uncommitted attempt at this same entry** (login UI skeleton, phone normalisation, Supabase SSR clients, `proxy.ts`, migrations, edge functions) — read and audited it rather than starting over; most of it was sound, but two real defects were found and fixed rather than left standing: (1) a stale `auth-send-sms-hook` Edge Function + `[auth.hook.send_sms]` config wired GoTrue to route delivery through a different, never-provisioned vendor (Thaibulksms) instead of the already-verified Vonage native-provider integration — removed entirely; the phone/IP rate-limit checks it hosted were moved into `console-auth-request-otp` (`supabase/functions/console-auth-request-otp/index.ts`), the only function now in the send path, which only gates and forwards to `signInWithOtp` and never sends SMS itself; (2) `@supabase/ssr`'s `createServerClient` defaults `cookieOptions.httpOnly` to `false` (its own library default, meant to also serve browser-client flows) — the existing `lib/supabase/server.ts`/`proxy.ts` never overrode it, so the session cookie would have shipped non-httpOnly, failing this entry's own acceptance criterion; fixed by passing `cookieOptions: { httpOnly: true }` explicitly in both, and moving OTP verification into a Server Action (`login/actions.ts`, `"use server"`) so the Set-Cookie header genuinely comes from Next.js's server response — the browser Supabase client (`lib/supabase/client.ts`) is never used to call `verifyOtp`/`signInWithOtp` for exactly this reason (JS can't set an httpOnly cookie via `document.cookie` regardless of what's configured). **Built:** `apps/console/src/app/console/login/page.tsx` (server component, reads `?next=` via `PageProps<"/console/login">`) + `LoginForm.tsx` (client, two-step phone→OTP flow, 60s resend cooldown per `interaction_spec.md`, auto-advance/paste OTP boxes via the pre-existing `OtpInput.tsx`, auto-submit on the 6th digit) + `login/actions.ts` (`verifyOtpAction`, open-redirect-guarded `next`) + `lib/requestOtp.ts` (direct browser→Edge-Function fetch, deliberately NOT proxied through a Server Action, so the function sees the merchant's real IP for its 20/IP/hour check rather than Vercel's). Copy: the wrong-OTP/unknown-number message is `interaction_spec.md`'s exact string (`รหัสไม่ถูกต้อง กรุณาลองใหม่`, line 37, identical for both cases per its own rule); `state_matrix.md` has no login/OTP section at all (it only enumerates post-auth order/menu states), so the remaining UI copy (labels, button text, phone-format validation message) was written directly in Thai per the WBS prompt's "all copy in Thai" instruction rather than sourced from a spec that doesn't cover this screen — flagging this rather than silently treating it as spec-backed. `packages/db/migrations/0023_merchant_auto_provision.sql`/`0024_auth_attempts.sql` mirrored byte-identical into `supabase/migrations/` (`diff -q` confirmed, matching the existing 0022 precedent). **`packages/db/seed.sql` had a real, previously-undetected bug against the new trigger**: it explicitly inserted a `merchants` row after inserting into `auth.users`, but 0023's `AFTER INSERT` trigger now creates that row first (same transaction) — the seed's own insert then hit `merchants_auth_user_id_key` (23505) on every `db reset`. Fixed by reading back the trigger-assigned `id` instead of inserting a second time; found and fixed by actually running `supabase db reset`, not by inspection. **`proxy.ts` not `middleware.ts`**: verified against `node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../file-conventions/middleware.md` — Next.js 16 deprecated the `middleware.js` convention in favour of `proxy.js`, identical behaviour, renamed file/export; the file's own header comment documents this. **Live-verified end-to-end, not just built** (Docker + local Postgres were reachable this session, superseding this doc's earlier "no Postgres reachable" caveat): `supabase db reset` clean across all 25 migrations + fixed seed; exactly one `merchants` row provisioned (`select count(*) ... = 1`, `phone`/`subscription_tier` correct); the trigger's `on conflict (auth_user_id) do nothing` re-run directly at the SQL level confirmed idempotent (`INSERT 0 0`, count stays 1); `console-auth-request-otp` exercised over real HTTP via `supabase functions serve` — 5 requests for one phone number all pass the rate limiter, the 6th returns `429` with the identical generic error (WBS's own "6th OTP request within an hour is rejected" test, satisfied directly), a different phone number is unaffected (separate bucket), malformed/non-E.164 input is rejected before ever touching the rate limiter or GoTrue, CORS preflight returns the expected headers. Added `[auth.sms.test_otp]` to `supabase/config.toml` (local-Docker-only, no effect on hosted projects, documented in-file as a test harness) mapping the seeded demo merchant's phone to a fixed code, because GoTrue refuses all phone-OTP flows with `phone_provider_disabled` when zero providers are enabled locally, even ones only used via `test_otp` — this unblocked the full loop for real testing. Installed Playwright's Chromium locally and drove the actual rendered page end-to-end (temporary script, not committed): typed `081-111-1111`, reached step 2, filled the 6 OTP boxes (auto-submit fired), landed on `/console`, confirmed the `next` param round-tripped, confirmed the session cookie (`sb-127-auth-token`) is genuinely `httpOnly: true`/`sameSite: Lax`, confirmed the session survives a page reload, and confirmed visiting `/console/login` again while authenticated redirects to `/console` per `proxy.ts`'s own logic — this is the acceptance criterion ("reaches `/console`") verified for real, not asserted from reading the code.
