Did not touch `apps/shop`; did not wire `packages/ui`'s generated tokens/components/Tailwind preset into `apps/console` (its `globals.css` still uses the pre-existing ad hoc `cream`/`ink`/`brand` theme that `OtpInput.tsx` already established before this dispatch) — the architect's own `docs/ui/design_system_port_design.md` §3.2 explicitly deferred that wiring to "the WBS entry that actually builds each app's shell," and doing it here would have meant resolving an unrelated open question (the generated `tailwind-preset.ts`'s default export shape doesn't match Tailwind v4's `@config` expectations as-is) outside this entry's scope; flagging it rather than silently leaving it undocumented. **qa_engineer leg (this update) — full WBS 4.1 testing block written and run for real against the local stack (`supabase db reset`, `supabase functions serve --no-verify-jwt`, `next dev`), not asserted from reading code.** New/updated test files: `apps/console/src/lib/phone.test.ts` (unit, all three input formats + edge cases), `scripts/check-shop-has-no-auth.mjs` + `supabase/functions/_tests/shop_no_auth_scan.test.ts` (the WBS prompt's own item 5, the one gap explicitly left open by the engineer leg — apps/shop is clean today, detection proven against planted violations of all three kinds), `supabase/functions/_tests/console_auth_request_otp.test.ts` (real HTTP: 5/phone/hour and 20/IP/hour both independently proven, 6th/21st request gets 429, separate-bucket isolation, pre-rate-limit input validation), `supabase/functions/_tests/otp_verify_identical_error.test.ts` (byte-identical GoTrue error for wrong-code-on-known-number vs any-code-on-unknown-number, status/message/code all equal), `packages/db/tests/merchant_auto_provision.test.ts` (exactly-one-row + replayed-insert idempotency + two-users-get-two-rows, real trigger, real Postgres), `apps/console/tests/e2e/otp-login.e2e.test.ts` (real Chromium via `playwright`, real Server Actions, real httpOnly cookie assertion, real reload-persists-session, real unauthenticated-redirect-with-?next=, and a real genuinely-expired-but-well-formed-JWT redirect for WBS 4.2's "expired session" criterion — NOT a literal real SMS send, which this environment cannot do and which the manual step + engineer's hosted curl proof already cover; drives the local `[auth.sms.test_otp]` substitution instead, documented in-file). **Result: 121 real assertions across `packages/db` (66/66 relevant + 1 opt-in skip), `supabase/functions/_tests` (36/36), and `apps/console` (20/21 — see defect below) all pass and are independently repeatable back-to-back** (re-ran each suite twice; auth_attempts self-cleans via the same local-only HMAC salt the app uses). **Two real, previously-undetected bugs found by actually running the suite, not by inspection, neither worked around in the tests:** (1) `auth.users.email_change` (distinct from `email_change_token_new`) has no table default and was missing from `createWellFormedAuthUserFixture`'s explicit `''` list (WBS 3.7's own fixture) — GoTrue's `/auth/v1/user` 500s with `sql: Scan error on column index 8, name "email_change": converting NULL to string is unsupported` for any auth.users row inserted via raw SQL without it; fixed in the test fixture (`supabase/functions/_tests/helpers/auth-user-fixture.ts`) since it's QA-owned, but **`packages/db/seed.sql` has the identical gap and was NOT fixed here** (out of qa_engineer's remit) — a fresh `db reset` followed directly by any console-* call against the seeded demo merchant, with no prior OTP verify in between to incidentally heal the column, will 500 the same way; flagging for engineer. (2) **`apps/console/src/proxy.ts`'s `supabase.auth.getUser()` call has no try/catch — a session cookie that isn't decodable at all as `@supabase/ssr`'s `base64-<base64(JSON)>` format (cookie corruption, a manual edit, a future session-format change) crashes the guard with an unhandled 500 (`Invalid UTF-8 sequence` from `stringFromBase64URL`) instead of failing closed to `/console/login`.** This is distinct from and narrower than "expired session" — a genuinely expired-but-well-formed JWT IS handled correctly today (clean 307 to `/console/login?next=...`, confirmed both via the e2e suite and directly via `curl`), so WBS 4.1/4.2's own "expired session redirects to login" criterion holds; this is specifically about undecodable cookie *data*, a realistic-but-narrower failure mode RL-3's "deny by default" framing doesn't explicitly cover. Left as a deliberately-failing regression test (`otp-login.e2e.test.ts`'s `[KNOWN DEFECT ...]` case, asserts the correct redirect behavior, currently fails with `500 not to be 500`) rather than silently loosened — this is the one test file in the 21-test e2e suite not currently green, and that failure is expected/intentional, not flaky. Also fixed, as a necessary precondition to running ANY of the above for real: WBS 4.1's new `on_auth_user_created_provision_merchant` trigger broke three PRE-EXISTING QA fixtures from WBS 3.5/3.6/3.7 that each did their own `insert into merchants (...)` immediately after inserting into `auth.users` (now colliding with the trigger's own row, `23505` on every run) — `packages/db/tests/helpers/rls-fixture.ts`, `packages/db/tests/schema.test.ts` (5 call sites), and `supabase/functions/_tests/helpers/auth-user-fixture.ts`, all switched to `on conflict (auth_user_id) do update ... returning id` (same fix shape as the engineer leg's own seed.sql fix for the identical regression). Also updated `rls.test.ts`'s pinned table count/name list from 18 to 19 (WBS 4.1 added `auth_attempts`) and added real RLS adversarial coverage for `auth_attempts` itself (zero access via anon or authenticated, same zero-policy shape as `job_queue`, service_role-proves-row-exists cross-check) — a new sensitive table shipping with no adversarial test of its own would have been a real gap in exactly the suite whose job is to catch that. `pnpm --filter @brewledger/console typecheck/lint`, `pnpm --filter @brewledger/functions-tests typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db typecheck`, and `pnpm lint:boundary` all clean on the new/changed files. Not run: `pnpm typecheck` at the repo root (pre-existing, unrelated `packages/db`/Prisma failure already noted in the 3.11 row). **engineer leg (this update) — both defects qa_engineer found above are fixed, real-verified, not just read:** (1) `apps/console/src/proxy.ts`'s `supabase.auth.getUser()` call wrapped in try/catch — any failure to establish a session (expired token, the existing working case, *or* an undecodable/corrupt cookie, the new case) now collapses to the same clean 307 redirect to `/console/login?next=...` instead of the undecodable-cookie path throwing through to an unhandled 500; the already-working expired-token path is untouched. Verified two ways: re-ran `otp-login.e2e.test.ts` for real (`supabase db reset`, `supabase functions serve --env-file supabase/functions/.env`, `next dev`) — all 5 tests pass including the `[KNOWN DEFECT ...]` case, which now asserts the redirect instead of the 500, with no regression on the sibling expired-session test; and independently repeated qa_engineer's own `curl -b "sb-127-auth-token=base64-garbage-not-a-real-session-jwt" http://localhost:3000/console/orders` repro directly, which now returns `307` to `/console/login?next=%2Fconsole%2Forders` instead of `500`. (2) `packages/db/seed.sql`'s `auth.users` insert for the demo merchant now sets `email_change: ''` explicitly, mirroring the exact fix already applied in QA's own fixture (`supabase/functions/_tests/helpers/auth-user-fixture.ts`). | 4.2 | Merchant Session, Roles and Route Guards | 4 | done | engineer, then qa_engineer, then redline_reviewer (RL-3 touch, Pattern B — no schema change) | engineer | redline_reviewer | (uncommitted at time of writing) | Scope narrower than the WBS dictionary's illustrative prompt, deliberately: exploration found most of the backend guard (`supabase/functions/_shared/console/auth.ts`'s `verifyConsoleRequest`/`withConsoleAuth`) already built ahead of schedule during WBS 3.7/4.1, and `docs/api/surfaces_design.md` §5 already specs `ConsoleContext` with no `tier`/`absorbGatewayFee` field — `absorbGatewayFee` is a dead leftover from the retired Prisma model / withdrawn gateway-fee plan (superseded by direct merchant-owned PromptPay, ADR-008), no backing column exists. Followed the architect's design doc over the dictionary's illustrative snippet rather than "fixing" the omission back in. **Built this entry:** `assertOwnedStore(ctx, storeId)` + `ForbiddenStoreError` in `auth.ts` — the WBS's central rule ("no console handler may accept a client-supplied `store_id` and trust it, never silently substitute") — wired into `withConsoleAuth`'s catch block as an empty-body 403, so a handler calls it inline with no per-handler try/catch needed, same "hard to get wrong" shape as the existing 401 path. `qa-console-auth-probe/index.ts` extended to accept an optional `store_id` (query or body) and call the assertion, giving the test suite a real endpoint before any business `console-*` function exists. Console client side: `packages/shared/src/merchant.ts` (`MerchantCtx`/`MerchantStoreSummary` types — no cost/margin/profit/stock field), `apps/console/src/lib/merchant.ts` (`resolveMerchantCtx()`, server-side resolver reusing the same `auth_store_ids()` RPC RLS and the Edge guard both use), `apps/console/src/lib/MerchantProvider.tsx` (`"use client"` context + `useMerchant()`), `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/layout.tsx` (new route-group layout, sibling of `login/` so login never triggers a merchant lookup pre-auth; `proxy.ts`'s existing `/console/:path*` matcher covers it with no changes). No authenticated page was built under `(app)/` — deliberately out of scope, WBS 4.3 is the first real consumer; `/console` will 404 post-login until then, flagged not silently left. **Three real defects found and fixed across this entry's qa_engineer/redline_reviewer passes, none self-certified:** (1) qa_engineer's `tenant_isolation.test.ts` caught `verifyConsoleRequest` mapping the `auth_store_ids()` RPC result as `{auth_store_ids: string}[]` row objects when the SQL function is `returns setof uuid` and supabase-js's `.rpc()` actually returns a plain `string[]` — every real store id became `undefined`, so `assertOwnedStore` rejected a merchant's own store on every request; not an RL-3 leak (RLS re-derives the same function server-side independently) but a hard block on the entry's own central mechanism. Fixed in `auth.ts` (`storeIds: (storeIds ?? []) as string[]`). (2) A first `redline_reviewer` pass, fresh session, found the identical bug duplicated in the second, independently-written implementation, `apps/console/src/lib/merchant.ts` line 37 — same fix applied there; the zero-store case had accidentally worked, which is why hand-testing never caught it, so qa_engineer added `apps/console/src/lib/merchant.test.ts` (mocked-client unit tests, 8 cases) with the store-owning-merchant path as the explicit regression — verified to actually catch the bug by temporarily reverting the fix, confirming the test failed, then restoring. (3) That same first review pass also flagged a LOW: no `import/no-restricted-paths` eslint zone protected `apps/shop` from `packages/shared/src/merchant.ts` the way `admin.ts` already gets a zone (type itself is clean, but nothing structural stopped a future RL-3-sensitive field from riding along once `apps/shop` gains a real `@brewledger/shared` dependency). The first attempted fix (add a zone entry) passed `lint:boundary` but a second review pass proved via empirical ESLint probes (real untracked files, `git status` confirmed clean before/after) that it was functionally inert: `merchant.ts` was still barrel-exported through `packages/shared/src/index.ts`, and `import/no-restricted-paths` matches on the resolved file of the import specifier, not on where a re-exported symbol originates — the one realistic import route (`@brewledger/shared`) sailed through untouched. Re-fixed properly by removing the barrel export (mirroring `admin.ts`'s existing non-export treatment) and switching `apps/console`'s import to the direct `@brewledger/shared/dist/merchant` subpath; while fixing this the engineer independently discovered the *same* symlink-resolution blind spot (`eslint-import-resolver-node`'s `preserveSymlinks: true` default) also silently neutered the pre-existing `admin.ts` zone against package-specifier imports, not just the new merchant zone, and closed both by adding `preserveSymlinks: false` to the shared resolver config — a third review pass independently reproduced the causal chain (a controlled before/after ESLint run with `preserveSymlinks` toggled) rather than trusting the explanation, and confirmed both zones now genuinely block the realistic `@brewledger/shared/src/...` package-specifier route. **One accepted, non-blocking residual gap, logged as a fast-follow, not fixed in this entry:** neither the `merchant.ts` nor the pre-existing `admin.ts` eslint zone covers the actual `dist/*` compiled subpath real consumers import (`apps/console` uses `@brewledger/shared/dist/merchant`, `worker` uses `.../dist/supabase/admin`) — only the `.ts` source path is a zone target. `apps/shop` has zero `@brewledger/*` dependencies today so this is currently unreachable, and `MerchantCtx` carries no forbidden field even in the worst case, so `redline_reviewer`'s judgment call was to not block this entry on it — recommended closing (add a second `from` entry per zone for the dist path, or a directory-level ban) before `apps/shop` takes its first real `@brewledger/shared` dependency. **Final verification, all real, not assumed:** `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`/`pnpm lint:boundary` clean across all workspaces; `scripts/test-import-boundary-regression.sh` and `scripts/test-functions-import-boundary-regression.sh` both PASS; `pnpm --filter @brewledger/console test` 29/29; `pnpm --filter @brewledger/functions-tests test` 55/55, run against a real local `supabase start` + `supabase functions serve --no-verify-jwt` stack, not the suite's own designed skip path; forbidden-nag-phrase grep on `apps/console/` empty. `git status` skim confirmed no unexplained files changed beyond this entry's scope. |
| 4.3 | Store Profile Setup | 4 | done | engineer, then redline_reviewer (Pattern C, screen-only — a real schema/RLS gap surfaced mid-entry escalated to architect out-of-band, still Pattern A for that piece) | engineer | redline_reviewer | (uncommitted at time of writing) | First real authenticated page under `apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/` — the layout WBS 4.2 built had no consumer until this entry; `/console` no longer 404s post-login. **Built:** `.../settings/store/page.tsx` + `StoreProfileForm.tsx` + `actions.ts` (`saveStoreProfile` Server Action), `apps/console/src/lib/slug.ts` (flat per-character Thai→Latin transliteration table, not syllable-aware RTGS — the WBS's own words, "correctness matters less than a stable readable ASCII slug"; `slugify()` strips to `[a-z0-9-]`, never blank via a `cafe-`+random-suffix fallback), a new `Toggle` component in `packages/ui` (genuinely missing from the WBS 2.2 port), and wired `packages/ui`'s CSS into `apps/console` for the first time (found and fixed a real `@import` ordering bug in the package's own documented recipe along the way — tokens/components must precede `tailwindcss` or Tailwind's expanded rules push the Google Fonts import into an invalid position). Slug uniqueness is enforced by attempting the write and retrying on Postgres `23505` with a `-2`/`-3`… suffix, not a live pre-check SELECT — confirmed necessary, not just cheaper: an authenticated merchant's RLS-scoped client cannot see any other merchant's store row, published or not, so a pre-check would always return zero regardless of a real collision. **A CRITICAL schema/RLS defect was found mid-entry, not by this entry's own reviewer but by the implementing engineer, and correctly not self-fixed:** `merchant_rw_stores` (WBS 3.6) gated `stores` through `auth_store_ids()`, which itself queries `stores` to find rows the caller already owns — self-referential, so no merchant could ever INSERT a `stores` row, first or Nth, reproduced against a real local `supabase db reset`. Routed to `architect` per this repo's own rule (schema/RLS is never engineer-owned): root cause traced to `stores` being the only merchant-owned table carrying `merchant_id` directly, so it never needed `auth_store_ids()`'s join at all. Fix shipped as an additive migration, `packages/db/migrations/0026_stores_insert_bootstrap_fix.sql` (mirrored byte-identical in `supabase/migrations/`) — a new `auth_merchant_id()` helper reading only `merchants` (cannot be self-referential), and `merchant_rw_stores` rebuilt to key off `merchant_id in (select auth_merchant_id())`. `docs/db/rls_design.md` §2/§3 and `docs/security/rls.md` both updated to match; `0021_rls.sql` itself was deliberately left untouched (already applied everywhere — Supabase tracks migrations by filename, editing a shipped one breaks drift detection). Verified against a real local Postgres, not simulated: 10 manual scenarios by the implementing engineer (first-store insert succeeds, cross-merchant insert rejected, cross-tenant UPDATE/SELECT/DELETE and anon visibility unchanged), then a formal adversarial suite by `qa_engineer` through real `authenticatedClient()` HTTP calls (not the fixture's superuser bypass, which is exactly the blind spot that let the original bug ship undetected) — including an explicit, commented assertion that a merchant creating a *second* store is intentional current behavior (no DB-level cardinality constraint; the WBS's own glossary states "in MVP a merchant owns exactly one store" as a product/UX invariant enforced at the application layer, not schema — Phase 2 multi-branch needs no migration to lift it). **Two real defects from this entry's own first `redline_reviewer` pass, fixed and independently re-verified by a second pass, not self-certified:** (1) HIGH — the ported `.oc-sw` toggle hit target was 32px, below `interaction_spec.md`'s 44px minimum (the prototype's own CSS ranks below the interaction spec in `CLAUDE.md`'s precedence table when they conflict); fixed at the component level in `packages/ui` by splitting the interactive `<button>` (now `min-height: var(--tap-min)`) from the unchanged-pixel-for-pixel visual track/thumb, so every future consumer (WBS 4.4's menu availability switches included) inherits the fix. (2) MEDIUM — `saveStoreProfile` trusted a client-supplied `storeId` with no explicit membership check against `merchant.storeIds`, short of WBS 4.2's own rule that every console handler needs an explicit second layer beyond RLS; fixed with an `isOwnedStoreId()` guard that runs before any Supabase client call in the function, traced end-to-end by the second review pass to confirm no code path reaches the DB before it. A second, judgment-call MEDIUM (bundle the publish toggle into the single form save vs. persist it instantly on flip) was explicitly assessed as not a clear violation — the prototype models no save-timing semantics for this screen either way — and left as bundled, which also ties the flip to the same required-field validation a stray tap could otherwise bypass. **A final fresh-session `redline_reviewer` pass returned FAIL** on two smaller findings — both fixed directly (not via another engineer dispatch, since they were pure doc/comment corrections with no logic change) and re-verified PASS by a fourth, narrowly-scoped review pass: stale header comments in `actions.ts`/`page.tsx` still describing the RLS gap as unfixed/architect-owned after migration 0026 had already closed it (both now cite the migration and `auth_merchant_id()` directly), and six real shipped Thai strings (four field-validation messages, save-success, save-error) missing from `docs/design/state_matrix.md`'s new section (added verbatim, cross-checked byte-for-byte against the code). **Final verification, all real:** `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`/`pnpm lint:boundary` clean across all workspaces; 220/220 tests passing (1 correctly-skipped destructive RLS test) across `packages/db` (live Postgres), `apps/console` (mocked Supabase), and `packages/ui` (first test in that package — declared-CSS-parsing + `react-dom/server` rendering, since no jsdom/testing-library harness exists yet, flagged by `qa_engineer` itself as a coverage gap to upgrade later, not a defect); forbidden-nag-phrase grep on `apps/console/` empty; `git status` scope-checked against the full file list with no stray changes beyond a few expected `packages/ui` export/catalog companions. |

Plus **2 HIGH**: the confirmation checkbox wasn't gated on the QR having actually rendered (`canConfirm` checked shape-validity only, not `qrDataUrl`), and editing a live *published* store's PromptPay ID never unpublished it, silently leaving `is_published: true` with `promptpay_verified_at: null`. Plus **1 MEDIUM**: a genuine same-submission re-verify after an identifier edit was discarded (`identifierChanged` unconditionally nulled `promptpay_verified_at`, ignoring `input.verified`). **architect** designed the DB-level fix for CRITICAL+HIGH-2 as one migration, `0027_promptpay_publish_guard.sql` (present in both `packages/db/migrations/` and `supabase/migrations/` — the CLI only reads the latter, a repo convention the engineer leg had to discover): a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger forcing `is_published := false` whenever it would otherwise be `true` with `promptpay_verified_at IS NULL` (fires on every write path, no app-layer change required), plus a `NOT VALID`→validated CHECK constraint as an inert backstop for when triggers are suppressed (e.g. `session_replication_role = replica`). confirmed (`supabase/config.toml`'s `[db.seed] sql_paths = ["../packages/db/seed.sql"]`) that a real, already-correct, gateway-free raw-SQL seed (`packages/db/seed.sql`, WBS 3.5) already exists and is the one `supabase db reset` actually runs — so the fix is deletion of the dead Prisma seed, not a rewrite of it as the report suggested; confirmed via `grep -rln "@brewledger/db"` that every real importer (apps/console pages, `packages/shared/src/serializers/public.ts`, the db test suite) uses only the `@brewledger/db/types` subpath (the generated `Database` type), never the package's bare/`main` export, so the Prisma-client singleton in `packages/db/src/index.ts` was equally dead and safe to replace with a type-only re-export. Deleted `packages/db/prisma/` (schema.prisma), `packages/db/seed.ts`, `packages/db/.env.example` (referenced deleted `apps/api` and the removed Prisma CLI, and nothing in the repo actually loads it — no `dotenv` call anywhere in `packages/db`). Rewrote `packages/db/src/index.ts` to `export type { Database, Json } from "./types"` only. Removed from `packages/db/package.json`: `@prisma/client` dependency, `prisma`/`ts-node` devDependencies (ts-node had no other caller once `seed.ts`'s `prisma.seed` config block was removed), `prisma:generate` script. Removed from root `package.json`: `db:migrate`/`db:seed`/`db:generate`/`db:validate` (kept `db:reset`/`db:push`/`db:diff`/`db:types`, all already Supabase-CLI-native; `db:reset` already re-seeds via `seed.sql` automatically, so no replacement `db:seed` script was needed). Removed the `onlyBuiltDependencies: [@prisma/client, @prisma/engines, prisma]` block from `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (now empty, deleted entirely rather than left as `[]`). Removed CI's `DATABASE_URL` env (Prisma-only), "Generate Prisma client" (`db:generate`), and "Validate Prisma schema" (`db:validate`) steps from `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — nothing else in CI needed either. Widened `rl1_no_gateway_fee_columns.test.ts`'s first query to scan all of `public` (dropped the `table_name in (...)` clause), matching `rl1_no_bank_details.test.ts`'s convention and the acceptance criterion's actual wording; re-verified myself by grepping all 28 migration files for the forbidden pattern directly — zero matches, so the widened net introduces no false-positive risk. Also fixed, as directly-caused staleness rather than scope creep: `docs/data_dictionary.md`'s pointer from the deleted `prisma/schema.prisma` to `packages/db/migrations/*.sql`; `eslint.config.mjs`'s header comment describing `packages/db` as "Prisma client + cost/margin schema" (now "generated Supabase schema types + cost/margin"); `docs/adr/008-direct-promptpay.md`'s "Consequences / follow-ups" note, which explicitly tracked this exact cleanup as outstanding and previously said "not this entry's [scope]" — updated to record it as resolved here, under 4.8. **Deliberately left untouched, flagged rather than fixed**: `packages/shared/src/supabase/client.ts` and `admin.ts` both carry a `TODO` reading "once packages/db no longer pulls Prisma into anything a browser bundle could reach, wire the real `Database` type via a type-only subpath export" — that blocking condition is now true, but actually wiring it touches RL-3-sensitive shared client code imported by both `apps/shop` and `apps/console`, which is a real change deserving its own reviewed entry, not a drive-by edit inside a Prisma-deletion fix pass. `docs/db/schema_design.md` and `docs/api/surfaces_design.md` still reference `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma` by path in several places — left as-is since those are architect's WBS 3.5/3.7 design documents narrating what was ported *from*, past tense, historically accurate at time of writing; not live pointers the way `data_dictionary.md`'s was. **`docs/poc_readiness.md` acceptance criterion**: N/A — this file has never existed anywhere in this repo's history (confirmed by a repo-wide search); there is no real cost-projection content to backfill it with, so this criterion is explicitly N/A rather than silently omitted. `packages/db/.env.example`'s deletion and `packages/shared`'s unresolved Prisma-reference comments are pre-existing/adjacent findings, not part of the redline_reviewer's CRITICAL/MEDIUM/LOW list, surfaced here for completeness. **Verification, all run for real**: `pnpm install` (non-frozen, to regenerate the lockfile after the dependency removal) — 45 packages dropped, confirmed the only remaining `prisma` hit in `pnpm-lock.yaml` is `@prisma/instrumentation`, a transitive dependency of `@sentry/node`'s generic auto-instrumentation, unrelated to this repo's own (now fully removed) Prisma usage; Payload is `buildPublicStoreUrl(slug)` — a new export in `lib/slug.ts` promoting the `"brewledger.app"` literal already hardcoded in WBS 4.3's `StoreProfileForm.tsx` into one shared constant (`PUBLIC_STORE_HOST`) both screens now import, since no env var for the public host exists anywhere in the repo and `apps/shop`'s `/s/[slug]` route isn't built yet to cross-check against. QR: `qrcode` npm package, error correction `M`, 1024×1024, quiet zone. Print sheet (A5/A6) renders entirely via offscreen `<canvas>` at 300 DPI (store name, QR, URL, Thai CTA) then embeds as one image into `jspdf` — deliberately avoids jsPDF's own text API, whose built-in fonts don't cover Thai glyphs. No design-doc coverage existed for this screen (state_matrix/interaction_spec/component_inventory all silent) — engineer authored a new state_matrix.md section per CLAUDE.md's precedence rule (same posture as WBS 4.4's self-authored empty states) rather than inventing an undocumented visual language. **redline_reviewer's full audit** (RL-3 is this entry's Red Line Touch, so no fast-path) confirmed the QR/PDF/PNG paths all derive from the one `buildPublicStoreUrl` call with no drift, no forbidden RL-2 phrases, correct error-correction/quiet-zone, and print-sheet Thai text genuinely canvas-rendered — but found **1 MEDIUM**: the unpublished-store QR was only CSS-dimmed (`opacity-40 grayscale`) while the `<img>` still held a fully valid, scannable base64 QR in the DOM — copyable via "copy image address" even though the app's own buttons were correctly disabled. **engineer** fixed it by gating QR generation itself behind a new `shouldGenerateQr(store)` helper: the encoder is never called for an unpublished store, and the unpublished branch renders a plain inert placeholder `<div>` with no `<img>`/data URL anywhere in the DOM, rather than a dimmed real one — chosen over raster-dimming because a dimmed-but-real QR matrix is still decodable with enough contrast correction, while "never encode it" is trivially provable and less code. **qa_engineer** independently reran the full suite (110/110 green), hand-verified the unpublished markup contains no `<img`/`data:image`, and closed a coverage gap the engineer's own test missed — added an assertion that the PNG/PDF download buttons actually carry `disabled` while unpublished. **redline_reviewer's targeted re-check** traced the published→unpublished transition case by hand (a store that was live, then closed, in the same component instance) and confirmed three independent gates — the render ternary, the download handlers, and `actionsDisabled` — all key off `store.isPublished` directly rather than off any possibly-stale `qrDataUrl`, so no single missed reset can leak a working QR; closed the MEDIUM finding, no new issue introduced. Left **1 LOW**, not blocking: this repo has no jsdom, so the published→unpublished *transition* itself (as opposed to a from-scratch unpublished render) can't be exercised by an automated test — its safety currently rests on the reviewer's manual trace, not a test. Final checks: `pnpm test` 110/110, `pnpm typecheck` clean, `pnpm lint` 0 errors (pre-existing `no-img-element` warnings only), `pnpm lint:boundary` clean, RL-2 nag-phrase grep across `apps/console/` empty. |

No Supabase import anywhere in the file — the file's own top comment states why: an `orders` row per abandoned browse would fill the 500 MB free-tier DB and corrupt WBS 5.3's slot-quota count, so "no order row created while browsing" holds by construction. Used a small custom store, not Zustand — not a dependency of `apps/shop` today and nothing here needed one. `apps/shop/src/lib/cartValidation.ts` — standalone, pure `revalidateCart(lines, freshMenu)`, diffing each cart line's item price/availability and each option's price/existence against a fresh `fetchPublicMenu` response; each `CartDiffReason` carries its originating `lineIndex` so the UI can target the right line for an accept/remove action; matches `interaction_spec.md`'s own rule ("an item that goes unavailable while in a cart is flagged at checkout, not silently removed"). `apps/shop/src/components/ItemOptionsSheet.tsx` — bottom sheet (ported `.cw-sheet`/`.cw-scrim`/`.cw-opt`/`.cw-optg`/`.cw-step` markup from `sheet()`), Escape-to-close added (a11y, no prototype equivalent), footer button disabled until all groups satisfied. `apps/shop/src/components/MenuItemRow.tsx` — root element changed `<div>`→`<button>` (matches the prototype's own `scMenu()` markup, `<button class="cw-item">`; WBS 5.1 used a div since nothing was tappable yet) so tapping opens the sheet; `MenuList.tsx` threads an `onItemTap` prop through. Split into two client components rather than one, deliberately: `MenuOrdering.tsx` (MenuList + sheet, renders inside `.cw-body`) and `CartBar.tsx` (the sticky `cw-cartbar`, renders as a sibling of `.cw-body` in `page.tsx`) — nesting the cart bar inside `.cw-body` would have inherited that container's padding and broken the prototype's edge-to-edge sticky bar; both read the same `useCart(slug)` external store independently and stay in sync with no context provider needed. `apps/shop/src/app/s/[slug]/cart/page.tsx` — client component (sessionStorage-backed cart can't be Server Component data), `useParams()` for the slug per Next 16's client-param convention (see `apps/shop/AGENTS.md`'s "this is not the Next.js you know" notice — checked `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/04-functions/use-params.md` directly rather than assuming Next 15 App Router shape). Empty state via `packages/ui`'s `EmptyState`. Proceed button re-fetches `fetchPublicMenu(slug)`, runs `revalidateCart`; on `ok`, routes to `/s/[slug]/checkout` (WBS 5.4, not built yet — this intentionally 404s today, per the dispatch's own scoping note, not stubbed); on a diff, shows one notice per changed line (`ใช้ราคาใหม่`/`ลบ` for a price change, `ลบ`-only for something no longer sellable) and re-checks locally against the cached fetch every time the cart changes, auto-proceeding once every diff is resolved. **`packages/ui/src/components.css`**: ported the remaining WBS-5.2-only `/design/Customer Web.html` selectors (`.cw-scrim`, `.cw-sheet`, `.cw-sheet-scroll`, `.cw-base`, `.cw-optg`, `.cw-opts`, `.cw-opt`(+`.is-on`), `.cw-step`, `.cw-sheet-foot`, `.cw-line`(+`-top`/`-bot`), `.cw-total`, `@keyframes up`) — literal port, same discipline as the WBS 5.1 block; one small addition beyond a literal port, flagged for review: `.cw-opt[disabled]` (dimmed/inert styling for a multi-select option at its group's cap), which the prototype has no rule for since its demo data never needed a disabled option button — modeled on the existing `.btn[disabled]` pattern already in this file. **`docs/design/state_matrix.md`**: added "ตัวเลือกรายการ (sheet)" and "ตะกร้า `/s/{slug}/cart`" sections (cited against `customer-web.js` line-for-line where the copy is sourced from the prototype; the "Stale cart at proceed" state is authored fresh, no prototype equivalent, citing `interaction_spec.md`'s flag-don't-silently-remove rule). **`apps/shop/src/lib/copy.ts`**: all new constants cite `state_matrix.md` per the file's own stated convention; money values in diff messages are composed as JSX around `PublicMoneyValue` (never hand-formatted into a string) per CLAUDE.md's money rule — `copy.ts` only exports the surrounding Thai text fragments (e.g. `priceChangedDiffPrefix(name)`), not a fully formatted sentence. **Design decisions not fully specified by the WBS, made here and flagged for review:** (1) the prototype's sheet pre-selects a default option per group (`opts:{temp:'ice',sweet:'s100'}`) — that default is the demo data's own item-specific hardcoding, not a real rule; the real sheet starts with nothing selected and gates add-to-cart on `minSelect` instead. (2) a revalidation diff resolves at the whole-line granularity (accept a new price, or remove the entire line) rather than per-option — simpler than reopening the sheet to reconfigure just one option, and the WBS's own acceptance bar ("caught and surfaced, not silently charged") doesn't require finer granularity. (3) option price deltas in the sheet render via `PublicMoneyValue` with `decimals={0}` (e.g. `+฿5`) rather than the prototype's bare unlabelled number (`+5`, no currency symbol) — chosen to keep every money value going through `PublicMoneyValue` (CLAUDE.md's money rule) rather than hand-formatting a raw number, at the cost of a small, deliberate fidelity deviation. **Test infra check (per this dispatch's own instruction to verify before inventing anything):** `apps/shop/package.json` has no test runner configured (no `vitest`/`jest` devDependency, no `test` script) — did not add one. The "no order row is created while browsing" acceptance criterion is satisfied by construction, not by a runtime-asserted test: `cart.ts` has zero Supabase imports and the cart page's only network call (`fetchPublicMenu`, a read) only fires on the proceed button, which routes to the not-yet-built `/checkout` rather than writing anything. WBS 8.2 owns this screen's E2E coverage per `.claude/agents/WORKFLOW.md`'s Pattern C. **Verification:** `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shop lint`, `pnpm lint:boundary`, `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shop build` all clean; `node scripts/check-route-bundle-size.mjs` — `/s/[slug]`'s own JS ~81 KB gzipped (up from WBS 5.1's ~73 KB), still under the 100 KB budget; `pnpm scan:forbidden-fields`, `node scripts/scan-forbidden-fields-bundle.mjs`, `pnpm scan:forbidden-copy` all clean. Not pushed to `origin/main`, same `docs/ops/migration_notes.md` gate WBS 5.1 followed. **`redline_reviewer` audit (this update):** one HIGH finding — option price-delta chips used `PublicMoneyValue value={option.priceDeltaSatang} decimals={0}`, rounding any nonzero delta under 100 satang (e.g. a 10-satang / ฿0.10 option) down to a misleading "+฿0" directly above a footer total that correctly included the charge. Fixed in `c0ecce3`: dropped the `decimals={0}` override, so the chip now renders at `PublicMoneyValue`'s default `decimals=2` (satang round-trips exactly at 2 decimal places, matching the item base price and footer total already on the same sheet). Re-reviewed `c0ecce3`: diff is exactly the one line (`decimals={0}` removed), nothing else in `ItemOptionsSheet.tsx` touched — scope respected. `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shop typecheck` and `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shop lint` both clean. RL-3 boundary, money arithmetic elsewhere, Thai copy/forbidden phrases, tap targets, "no order row while browsing," revalidation acceptance bar, multi-select gating, and CSS fidelity were all verified in the original audit and are unaffected by this one-line change. One MEDIUM remains open as a cross-cutting follow-up, not a WBS 5.2 defect: the console's option-group editor never exposes `is_required`/`min_select`, so this entry's required-group gating (`areAllGroupsSatisfied`/`isGroupSatisfied`) is currently unreachable with real merchant-entered data — belongs to whichever WBS owns the console's menu editor. |

module unconditionally, including `log.ts`'s redaction regex/error text) — fixed by importing `@brewledger/shared/dist/{money,supabase/client}` directly instead of the barrel (established precedent (B) `packages/shared/src/log.ts`'s two comments naming `unit_cost_snapshot_satang` reworded to generic examples (prose-only, `COST_KEY` regex untouched); `packages/shared/package.json` gained an `exports` map covering all 18 real subpath imports found by grepping the whole repo (deliberately omitting `"."`, so the bare `@brewledger/shared` barrel no longer resolves); `eslint.config.mjs`'s `apps/shop` block gained a `no-restricted-imports` rule (bans `MoneyValue`/`MetricCard` from `@brewledger/ui` and the bare `@brewledger/shared` barrel) and one new `import/no-restricted-paths` zone banning `packages/shared/src/features.ts`. (C) New `scripts/scan-forbidden-fields-bundle.mjs` (no spec — engineer's own design) scans the real built `apps/shop/.next/static/**/*.{js,css}` for forbidden substrings; wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` after the build step, alongside `pnpm scan:forbidden-fields` (the pre-existing JSON-snapshot scan, itself never wired into CI before now) and `pnpm -r --if-present test` (so `forbidden_field_scan.test.ts` and every other suite actually run in CI for the first time). **Deviation, self-caught, flagged for redline_reviewer:** a literal "fail on any substring anywhere" bundle scan (as Finding C's task text describes) is permanently red against this app's real build — `margin`/`payload`/`profit`/`stock` all appear dozens of times as unavoidable vendor/framework text (React Fiber's `payload` update-queue field, DOM `marginTop`/`marginBottom` CSSStyleDeclaration properties React's Offscreen internals and Next's error overlay both set, `@supabase/auth-js`'s own "expired with margin of 90000s" message, `packages/ui`'s sanctioned `--c-profit` token name). Implemented a shape check instead: a hit only counts when the forbidden substring's enclosing identifier-shaped token is directly wrapped in matching quote marks (`"cost_satang":1800`-shaped — how a real serialized DTO leak would appear), which a plain CSS declaration or unquoted JS property access never is; verified this still catches a planted leak by hand. Two narrow, documented exemptions remain: the literal quoted token `out_of_stock` (the sanctioned `PublicMenuItem.availability` enum value) and the `marginTop`/`marginBottom`/etc. CSS-property-name pattern (React's `hasOwnProperty("marginTop")` check is genuinely quoted but is a DOM property name, not a data value). **Also found and fixed while wiring `pnpm -r test` into CI for the first time** (pre-existing gaps, unrelated to any of the 3 findings): `packages/ui/tsconfig.json` had no `exclude` for `*.test.tsx` (unlike `packages/shared/tsconfig.json`, which already excludes `*.test.ts`), so `pnpm build` compiled `Toggle.test.tsx` into `dist/`, where vitest picked up the compiled duplicate and failed on a missing `dist/src/components.css` — added the matching exclude. `apps/console/tests/e2e/otp-login.e2e.test.ts` is a Playwright-driven test against a live `next dev` server that does not self-skip when the server isn't running (unlike every other infra-dependent suite in this repo) and is separately rate-limited (5 logins/hour, one seeded test number) — split `apps/console/package.json`'s `test` script to `--exclude '**/*.e2e.test.ts'` and added `test:e2e` to still run it explicitly. **Verification, all clean:** `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:boundary`, `pnpm build` (all workspaces from a genuinely clean `dist/`-less state), `node scripts/check-route-bundle-size.mjs` (73 KB, unchanged), `pnpm scan:forbidden-fields`, `node scripts/scan-forbidden-fields-bundle.mjs`, and `pnpm -r --if-present test`. **One residual, not fixed (pre-existing, out of scope):** `packages/shared/tests/storage/storage.test.ts` fails in this sandbox specifically — not in a fresh CI runner — because this environment's long-lived local Supabase Postgres has accumulated stale fixture rows from earlier sessions (`merchants_auth_user_id_key` collision on a fixture re-insert); its own `beforeAll` correctly reports "unreachable → skip" on a truly fresh stack, and `ci.yml` never starts one, so this cannot reproduce in real CI. Also noted, separately pre-existing and unrelated: `pnpm typecheck` (root script only builds `@brewledger/shared` first, not `@brewledger/ui`) fails from a truly clean `dist/`-less checkout before any `build` step has run, because `apps/shop`/`apps/console` typecheck against `packages/ui`'s `dist/`; `pnpm build` itself is unaffected (pnpm's topological build ordering builds `packages/ui` first). **Final verification (this pass, commit `f1825d7`):** the flagged `typecheck` script gap was the sole open item — orchestrator applied the one-line fix specified (root `package.json`'s `typecheck` script now runs `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shared --filter @brewledger/ui build` before `pnpm -r typecheck`). redline_reviewer independently re-ran the clean-state repro (`rm -rf packages/ui/dist packages/shared/dist` then `pnpm typecheck` from that dist-less state) and confirmed exit 0; diffed `f1825d7` against `b048d03` and confirmed the change is exactly the specified one line with no other file touched; confirmed `git status` clean (no stray WIP). `release_pickup_slot` explicitly revoked from `anon`/`authenticated` (not just left ungranted — `0021_rls.sql`'s own `alter default privileges ... grant execute ... to anon, authenticated, service_role` would otherwise hand `anon` execute on it automatically, the exact trap `0025_auth_attempts_atomic_check.sql`'s own comment already documents finding once before) and granted to `service_role` only, since no legitimate caller exists yet (WBS 5.7/5.11 aren't built). `enqueue_generate_slots_job(store_id)` — a fourth, self-designed RPC letting the console's authenticated merchant session trigger "on demand when store hours change" (`job_queue` has RLS enabled with deliberately zero policies for any role per `docs/db/rls_design.md` §2.2, so an ordinary authenticated INSERT is rejected by RLS; this is the documented sanctioned bypass shape, ownership-checked via the same `auth_store_ids()` RLS itself is built on). **Worker** (`worker/src/handlers/slots.ts`, registered in `handlers/index.ts` as `generate_slots`): loops either one `job.store_id` (on-demand) or every store with `opens_at`/`closes_at` set (nightly), calling the SQL function per store. **On-demand trigger wired into WBS 4.3's `saveStoreProfile`** (`settings/store/actions.ts`) — fires `enqueue_generate_slots_job` best-effort after every successful save (logged, never fails the save itself); fires on every save rather than diffing old/new hours, since regeneration is idempotent and the simplicity was judged worth the harmless extra no-op call. **Edge Function** (`supabase/functions/public-reserve-slot/index.ts`): thin POST wrapper around `reserve_pickup_slot`, own local CORS block (not `_shared/public/response.ts`'s `jsonResponse`, whose `Access-Control-Allow-Methods` is hardcoded `GET, OPTIONS` — every other `public-*` function is a GET; this is the first POST, same local-CORS precedent `console-auth-request-otp` already set for the same reason). Zero rows -> `410 {error:"slot_full"}` (a full, closed, past, or nonexistent slot are all indistinguishable on purpose, so a caller can't probe for real slot ids via a 404). **Not yet called by any customer flow** — `SlotPicker.tsx` deliberately does NOT reserve on selection (would burn real capacity against browses that never convert, the same failure mode WBS 5.2's cart design already rejected for order rows); the real reservation call belongs inside WBS 5.4's `public-create-order` transaction, not built yet. This function exists now, standalone, so the primitive is real and independently testable ahead of 5.4. **Customer picker** (`apps/shop/app/s/[slug]/checkout/page.tsx` + `src/components/SlotPicker.tsx`): full `/checkout` screen per `docs/design/state_matrix.md`'s existing "เลือกเวลารับ" section and `design/customer-web.js`'s `scCheckout()` (grouped-by-hour slot buttons, `เหลือ N ที่` soft-urgency label only at ≤2 remaining, name field, "slot taken while typing" re-validation against `public-slots` on submit, CTA disabled until both a slot and a name are present) — the CTA's real destination (order creation, navigation to `/pay`) is explicitly a documented stub, since WBS 5.4/5.5 don't exist yet; no fake navigation to a route that doesn't exist was added. `groupSlotsByHour` added to `storeSchedule.ts` alongside the pre-existing WBS 5.1 functions. **Merchant capacity console screen** (`/console/settings/capacity`): per-slot capacity override (inline edit + save per upcoming slot) and a bulk-default control that persists `stores.default_slot_capacity` AND immediately bulk-updates every future open slot whose current `booked_count` doesn't exceed the new value — a slot already booked past the new, lower capacity is skipped (reported back as a count, not silently dropped or force-erroring the whole batch) rather than violating the pre-existing `check (booked_count <= capacity)`. that gap is a dispatch mistake (orchestrator's, not the reviewer's), corrected by re-dispatching directly against the real working directory. **Redline review, round 2 (fresh session, working directory, live-reproduced against a real Postgres via `supabase db reset --local`)** found **2 CRITICAL**, both fixed in this same pass: **(1)** migration `0028` existed only in `packages/db/migrations/`, never mirrored into `supabase/migrations/` (the directory `supabase db reset`/`db push` actually read) — reviewer reproduced this live, `db reset` silently applied through `0027` and skipped `0028` entirely, so every deliverable in this entry would 404/500 in any environment provisioned the documented way; fixed by copying the file byte-for-byte into `supabase/migrations/`, confirmed identical via `diff`, then re-ran `supabase db reset --local` and it now applies `0028` correctly. **(2)** `reserve_pickup_slot` was granted to `anon` (reasoned at the time as "mirrors `public_order_status`, the one write a customer's own request legitimately triggers") — reviewer correctly identified this as a live capacity-exhaustion hole: any unauthenticated caller can read every open slot id off the pre-existing `public-slots` GET endpoint and reserve all of them with no order ever created, and neither WBS 5.7 (expiry) nor 5.11 (cancellation) exists yet to release a stale hold, so the exhaustion is permanent — a direct inversion of this entry's own acceptance criterion. Fixed by making `reserve_pickup_slot` service_role-only (same posture as `release_pickup_slot`, revoked from `anon`+`authenticated` explicitly against `0021_rls.sql`'s standing default-privileges grant) and **deleting the `public-reserve-slot` Edge Function and its test entirely** rather than leaving a now-nonfunctional public stub — the reviewer's own recommended fix (a): hold the public HTTP surface until WBS 5.4 wraps reservation inside a real order-creation transaction with a matching expiry; the SQL-level atomicity guarantee doesn't need a public caller to prove it, and `pickup_slots.test.ts`'s concurrency/grant assertions were updated to match (`anon_can_reserve: false`, `service_role_can_reserve: true`) and re-run live, passing. **Everything else in the review passed** — RL-1 (no money touched), RL-2 (no forbidden phrases, no cost-zero-coalescing), RL-3 (checkout/SlotPicker import nothing from `apps/console`/`packages/costing`, never reference raw `capacity`/`booked_count`, `SlotPicker` confirmed to never call the reservation primitive), `enqueue_generate_slots_job`'s ownership check (live-tested against a second real merchant, correctly rejects), the console capacity actions' ownership scoping (both `.eq` guards plus the `isOwnedStoreId` check), migration additivity/re-runnability, the hand-edited `types.ts` entry, and the new `state_matrix.md` copy (checked string-for-string against `copy.ts`). **Post-fix re-verification, all real:** `supabase db reset --local` (0028 applies via the correct path — re-confirmed a second time after a Docker Desktop restart), `packages/db/tests/pickup_slots.test.ts` 10/10 against a DB provisioned that way, full `pnpm -r --if-present test` (394 tests, all green), `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`/`pnpm lint:boundary`/`pnpm lint:functions` all clean repo-wide. **`pnpm build` gap now closed** — the disk-full/Docker-unresponsive state noted earlier was an environment problem (Docker Desktop's WSL2 backend needed a restart, independent of this entry's code); once the user freed disk space and Docker was restarted, `pnpm build` ran clean end to end (both new routes present, `/s/[slug]/checkout` and `/console/settings/capacity`), `node scripts/check-route-bundle-size.mjs` (81.5 KB gzipped, under the 100 KB budget) and `node scripts/scan-forbidden-fields-bundle.mjs` (0 hits) both re-confirmed against the post-fix build output. **`pg_meta`/`rest`/`studio`/`edge-runtime` still segfault (exit 139) in this sandbox even with ample disk and a fresh Docker restart** — confirmed this is a genuine sandbox/platform limitation (likely a missing CPU instruction set in this specific virtualized environment for those particular Deno/Rust binaries), not a disk-space artifact as first suspected; `db`/`auth`/`kong`/`storage` remain healthy, which is what every test in this repo that needs live Postgres actually depends on. This means `apps/shop`'s Edge-Function-backed pages (including the new `/checkout`) cannot show real seeded data in THIS sandbox's browser preview — `dev` servers were started and smoke-tested (`/s/{slug}/checkout` → 200, `/console/settings/capacity` → 307 redirect-to-login, both correct given no reachable backend) but render their not-found/error states rather than live content. **Not marking `done`** — per this file's own Update rule, only the reviewing leg may do that, and the two CRITICAL fixes applied after round 2's report have not themselves been re-reviewed (though they are small, mechanical, live-verified against a real Postgres via the documented `db reset` path, and directly implement the reviewer's own stated fix). Recommend a third, short redline pass scoped only to the two changed spots (the grant flip on `reserve_pickup_slot` and the deleted `public-reserve-slot` Edge Function) before flipping Status to `done`. **Post-review fix (2026-08-21, engineer, `f58e265`):** a real UI bug was found live (Playwright, 480px viewport) against a real 7-day-ahead-generated dataset (562 future slots, `demo-cafe`) — `groupSlotsByHour` (`apps/shop/src/lib/storeSchedule.ts`) grouped purely by 2-digit hour-of-day with no day awareness, so `generate_pickup_slots_for_store`'s standing `days_ahead=7` plus `public-slots`' unfiltered future-slot list produced an unbounded wall of ~140 duplicate, unlabeled hour headers (~15,000px of scroll). Neither `state_matrix.md`, `interaction_spec.md`, nor `component_inventory.md` documents a multi-day picker UI — the delivered prototype (`design/customer-web.js`'s `scCheckout`) only ever renders one day's slots, and `state_matrix.md`'s own "All full" title (`วันนี้เต็มทุกช่วงเวลาแล้ว`, "today is completely full") already assumes a single-day default view. Fixed by adding `filterSlotsForToday` (day-scopes to the store-local calendar day before `groupSlotsByHour` runs; `groupSlotsByHour`'s own contract and existing tests untouched) and wiring `SlotPicker.tsx`'s `allFull`/all-full body to the day-filtered set and the real computed next-available label instead of a hardcoded `"พรุ่งนี้"`. No new component, no new Thai copy beyond the existing วันนี้/พรุ่งนี้ vocabulary. 4 new unit tests in `storeSchedule.test.ts` (21/21 passing), `pnpm --filter @brewledger/shop typecheck/lint/build` clean, `grep -rIl "unit_cost\|margin\|profit\|stock_level\|service_role" apps/shop/.next/static/` returns only the pre-catalogued CSS-margin/marginTop/auth-js noise (traced by hand, zero real hits — same posture WBS 5.4's own row already documents for this exact grep). Returns `PublicPaymentIntent` built field by field via `toPublicPaymentIntent`/`parseOrThrow`, never a spread of the RPC row (which also carries `id`/`order_id`/`status`, none of which cross the boundary). **DTO** (`packages/shared/src/serializers/public.ts`/`public.schema.ts`): `PublicPaymentIntent`/`toPublicPaymentIntent`/`publicPaymentIntentSchema` added at the trailing comment that already earmarked this addition; An independent, floating-point-free ground-truth formatter (pure integer/string arithmetic, no division, no `toFixed`) is cross-checked against the real encoder's tag 54 output for every integer satang value 1–2000 exhaustively, plus 11 realistic multi-digit odd order totals up to 99,999.99 THB. All pass — no rounding or float-representation drift anywhere in range.

**New — `packages/db/tests/promptpay_charge.test.ts`:** drives `create_payment_charge` (0030) directly against real Postgres, same posture as `checkout_create_order.test.ts`/`pickup_slots.test.ts`. Each guard condition as its own isolated test: order-not-found, order status `ACCEPTED`/`EXPIRED`/`CANCELLED` (three separate cases), store with `promptpay_id` null, and store with `promptpay_id` SET but `promptpay_verified_at` still null (the "unverified, not merely unconfigured" case distinct from the first) — all six assert zero rows returned AND zero `payments` rows inserted. A positive-control happy path proves the zero-row cases are the guard firing, not a broken function, and asserts the persisted `payee_alias` is exactly the store's own PromptPay ID (RL-1 evidence, at the DB layer) and `amount_satang` is read fresh from `orders.total_satang` (the RPC takes no amount parameter at all, so a client can never supply one). An `expires_at` freshness check confirms the 15-minute countdown is computed from call time, not derived from `orders.expires_at`. Append-only reissue is proven by calling the RPC twice and then a third time on the same still-`PENDING_PAYMENT` order: every call succeeds, every call inserts a distinct `payments` row, none are overwritten (no unique constraint on `payments.order_id`, confirmed behaviorally not just by reading the schema comment). A `has_function_privilege` grants check, mirroring `pickup_slots.test.ts`'s own style, confirms `anon`/`authenticated` cannot execute `create_payment_charge` and only `service_role` can. 11/11 pass.

**Not independently tested (documented limitation, not a silent skip):** `supabase/functions/public-create-charge/index.ts`'s own HTTP status-code branches (404/409/201) — this sandbox has no edge-runtime container (`docker ps` shows db/auth/kong/storage/etc. healthy, no `edge-runtime`), the same gap `checkout_create_order.test.ts` already documents for `public-create-order`. Every guard condition the Edge Function's branches depend on is proven at the RPC layer above; the wrapper itself has no branching logic beyond those guards plus this RPC call. The WBS's real-device banking-app scan (two Thai banking apps, correct payee, locked amount) is out of scope for automated testing — manual/deferred, not run.

| 5.7 | Order Status Lifecycle | 5 | done | A (architect -> engineer -> qa_engineer -> redline_reviewer) | architect, engineer, qa_engineer | redline_reviewer | (uncommitted) | Full audit, not fast-path (new schema + touches WBS 5.6's payment-confirmation RPCs). **architect** (`docs/db/wbs_5_7_lifecycle_design.md`): the WBS prompt's literal `transitionOrder(tx, orderId, to, actor)` TS signature doesn't fit this codebase (Edge Functions never hold a raw Postgres transaction handle) — real guard is `transition_order()`, a `plpgsql security definer` function; `supabase/functions/_shared/orders/lifecycle.ts` is a thin RPC wrapper, not a second source of truth. **RL-1 "refund" resolved**: `transition_order` never moves money — leaving a paid state into `CANCELLED` sets `orders.refund_status = 'pending'`, a flag only; marking it `'done'` is WBS 5.11's job; `CANCELLED -> REFUNDED` blocked unless `refund_status = 'pending'`. Found a real gap: `console_confirm_payment`/`console_reject_payment` (0031) and `worker/src/handlers/expireOrders.ts` wrote `orders.status` directly — routing both through the guard made a hard dependency of this entry, not optional. **engineer**: `packages/db/migrations/0032_order_status_history.sql` (table + RLS, one `authenticated` SELECT policy scoped via `auth_store_ids()`, zero new anon policies — still exactly four anon SELECT policies repo-wide; `transition_order()` with the full ten-pair map, `errcode BL001` on every illegal pair including all 8 self-transitions); `0033_route_payment_confirmation_through_lifecycle.sql` (`console_confirm_payment`/`console_reject_payment` now delegate to `transition_order()`, `console_reject_payment`'s actor derived via existing `auth_merchant_id()` helper, no signature change); `supabase/functions/_shared/orders/lifecycle.ts` (thin wrapper, catch-and-compare idempotency on `BL001`, matching 0031's existing double-tap pattern); `worker/src/handlers/expireOrders.ts` refactored from bulk UPDATE to a per-row loop through `transition_order`; `scripts/scan-order-status-bypass.mjs` + `pnpm lint:lifecycle` wired into CI, excluding test files/fixtures and the migrations directory (verified those exclusions don't cover real production code). All three migrations byte-identical between `packages/db/migrations/` and `supabase/migrations/`. **qa_engineer**: local Supabase stack was reachable this session (`npx supabase db reset` applied cleanly) — CLAUDE.md's "no Postgres reachable" caveat is stale here; ran everything against a real DB. New `packages/db/tests/order_lifecycle.test.ts`: full 54-illegal-pair matrix (incl. all 8 self-transitions) throws `BL001`; all 10 legal transitions fire side effects exactly once under both sequential and genuinely concurrent (`Promise.all`, 10 connections) retry; expiry sweep releases the slot, verified against real anon PostgREST visibility before/after; history rows correct on every transition; RLS adversarial (owner-only, not anon, not cross-store); `refund_status` flag-only via function-body text scan for balance/escrow/wallet/payout (none found); CI scanner catches injected violations, no false positives on legit read-filters/fixtures; all 9 existing WBS 5.6 regression tests still pass. **Found a real production bug** (not fixed by qa, flagged for engineer): the append-only trigger on `order_status_history` raised unconditionally on any DELETE/UPDATE with no exception for legitimate FK-cascade deletes — `order_id -> orders ON DELETE CASCADE` chained through `orders -> stores -> merchants -> auth.users` meant a merchant account could never be deleted once any of its orders had a status transition; broke teardown of two qa suites for real. **engineer fix round 1** (`0034_order_status_history_allow_cascade_delete.sql`): qa's proposed `pg_trigger_depth() = 0` guard was empirically wrong (depth is never 0 inside the trigger body — 1 direct, 2 cascade); used `pg_trigger_depth() > 1` instead. Found and fixed two more issues surfaced only once the trigger stopped masking them: `actor -> merchants ON DELETE SET NULL` also needed the exemption on UPDATE (`return new`, not `old`); a cascade-ordering hazard required `order_status_history_order_id_fkey` to become `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`. Verified live: direct mutation still blocked, real `DELETE FROM auth.users` cascades cleanly through all four tables with zero orphans. **redline_reviewer pass 1** found **1 HIGH**: the blanket `pg_trigger_depth() > 1` exemption doesn't distinguish "legitimate RI cascade" from "any nested trigger" — reproduced live by attaching an ordinary unrelated `AFTER INSERT` trigger on a scratch table whose body directly UPDATEd `order_status_history.to_status`; firing it at depth 2 let the tampering write through with no exception (requires DDL privilege to exploit, so not reachable by `anon`/`authenticated` today, but the trigger's own header comment overstates what it delivers). **engineer fix round 2** (`0035_order_status_history_narrow_cascade_exemption.sql`): narrowed the depth>1 exemption to require the FK's existence-check actually holds — DELETE only exempt if `OLD.order_id` no longer resolves in `orders`; UPDATE only exempt for the exact `NEW.actor IS NULL AND OLD.actor IS NOT NULL` shape AND `OLD.actor` no longer resolving in `merchants`; every other case, any depth, still raises. Added a permanent regression test reproducing the reviewer's exact scratch-trigger exploit, now blocked. **redline_reviewer pass 2** (targeted re-check, not a full re-audit): read the 0035 logic, confirmed it's a real structural invariant (can't be satisfied without the referenced row genuinely being gone) not security-through-obscurity; independently reran qa's regression test plus four of their own adversarial variants live via `docker exec ... psql` (wrong-shape UPDATE, forged actor-null with merchant still existing, DELETE with parent order still existing — all correctly blocked; direct `DELETE FROM orders` not via `auth.users` — cascades cleanly, confirming the fix isn't over-narrowed to only the auth.users path); confirmed no regression to the 0034 cascade-delete/direct-block/DEFERRABLE-FK findings. **Zero remaining findings. Marking `done`.** Full regression, final state: `packages/db` 172/172 (+1 unrelated pre-existing skip), `worker` 4/4, all against a real local Postgres. `pnpm lint:lifecycle` clean against the real repo. |

| 6.4 | Purchase Confirmation Screen (Manual) | 6 | done | C (engineer -> redline_reviewer; qa_engineer skipped per WORKFLOW's screen-entry default, but the WBS prompt's own "Tests" section was still written directly by this leg, matching this session's dispatch instructions) | engineer | redline_reviewer | uncommitted | Built `/console/expenses/{id}/review` (`apps/console/src/app/console/(app)/expenses/[id]/review/{page.tsx,ReviewForm.tsx,ConfirmedInvoiceView.tsx,actions.ts}`) and the atomic confirm transaction, `console_confirm_purchase_invoice` (`packages/db/migrations/0044_console_confirm_purchase_invoice.sql`, mirrored byte-identical to `supabase/migrations/`, confirmed via `scripts/check-migration-numbers.mjs`). **Data-flow decision (RPC vs. Edge Function vs. plain Server Action), disclosed**: this needed to be a `security definer` plpgsql RPC — not a plain multi-`.from()` Server Action — for exactly one reason: `job_queue` carries zero RLS policies for `authenticated` (0021), so the `cost_recalc` enqueue would 403 otherwise; every other table it touches already grants `authenticated` full RW under `store_id in (select auth_store_ids())`. Chose to call this RPC **directly from the Server Action** (`createClient().rpc(...)`, same shape as `inventory/page.tsx`'s existing `ingredient_stock_levels` call), **not** via a new `supabase/functions/console-*` Edge Function — WBS 5.6/5.9's order/payment actions route through Edge Functions, but that indirection is scoped to CLAUDE.md's own enumerated critical-path list (menu, slot check, order creation, QR issue, payment confirmation, order status); this is an inventory/costing write, not an order-lifecycle one, and the established precedent for this class of write in this console (`saveIngredient`, `archiveIngredient`, `ingredient_stock_levels`) is a direct RPC/`.from()` call. Flagged for redline_reviewer as an intentional divergence from the 5.x convention, not an oversight. **Division of labour inside the RPC, disclosed**: purchase-unit-to-base-unit conversion (`toBaseUnit`/`costPerBaseUnit`, WBS 6.5's `packages/costing/src/units.ts`) happens client-side in `ReviewForm.tsx` (needed anyway for the live "before confirm" preview) and is passed through as already-computed `qtyBaseUnit`/`unitCostSatang` — not re-derived in SQL, unlike `checkout_create_order`'s full server-side re-validation, because this caller is the merchant's own authenticated session writing their own store's data (the same trust boundary `saveIngredient` already operates inside), not an untrusted public browser. The RPC still independently re-verifies OWNERSHIP of every referenced ingredient id against `auth_store_ids()`. **Atomicity design**: two passes inside the function — pass 1 is pure shape validation (no DB reads, so a soft `{ok:false, VALIDATION_ERROR}` can safely early-return, since Postgres only rolls back a function's implicit transaction on an actual exception, never on a plain `RETURN`); pass 2 does the real writes, checking each mapped line's ingredient-ownership immediately before writing that line and `raise exception`-ing on a cross-store id — this is what makes an earlier line's already-executed writes in the SAME call roll back too, proven directly by a real test (below), not asserted from documentation. Idempotent double-tap guard mirrors `console_advance_order`'s own `{ok:true, already:true}` shape. **Scope boundary, disclosed**: implements the specific four writes this entry's own Claude Code Prompt step 4 lists (`purchase_invoices` update, `purchase_line_items` insert, `ingredients.current_unit_cost_satang` update, `stock_ledger` insert, `cost_recalc` enqueue) and documents the "latest purchase price, not weighted average" costing method decision inline (migration comment + a new section in `docs/db/schema.md`) since this transaction is where that WBS 6.6 decision is actually first applied — but does **not** build WBS 6.6's own separate `ingredient_cost_history` table, its dedicated `packages/costing/src/cost.ts` engine, or a real `cost_recalc` worker handler (still the pre-existing stub in `worker/src/handlers/index.ts` — this entry only enqueues the job with `{ingredientIds, sourceInvoiceId}`); 6.6/6.8/6.9's own remaining deliverables are left for their own dispatch, not silently absorbed here. **Screen**: image (if attached) with tap-to-expand full-screen zoom, still-editable vendor/date/name/qty/unit/unit-cost fields carried over from 6.1, a new per-line "หน่วยซื้อ" (purchase unit) selector filtered to the mapped ingredient's base-unit family (kg/g, L/ml, or piece/dozen/pack — pack only offered once a `pack_size` exists), a per-line ingredient-mapping `<select>` (exact-name match auto-selected, `+ สร้างวัตถุดิบใหม่` inline creation without leaving the screen via a new `createIngredientInline` action, `ไม่ผูก (บันทึกเป็นค่าใช้จ่ายทั่วไป)` as the always-safe default whenever no exact match exists — RL-2: never auto-forced), a live plain-Thai "before confirm" summary per changed line (`นมสด: ต้นทุนเปลี่ยนจาก 42 เป็น 45 บาท/ลิตร กระทบ 6 เมนู`, "กระทบ N เมนู" computed from a real `bom_lines` count, omitted when 0 or when cost is unchanged), and a `ยืนยัน` button that blocks (rather than silently drops) a line left mid-creation (`ingredientId === "__new__"` never silently falls back to "unmapped"). No OCR confidence scoring, confidence styling, or arithmetic-mismatch banner anywhere in this file, per the WBS's own explicit instruction and the Phase 6.0 deferral. Corrected `docs/design/state_matrix.md`'s `/console/expenses/{id}/review` section, which still described the deferred OCR-confidence UI (`ConfidenceField`, amber border, confidence-ordered focus) verbatim from the withdrawn plan — per CLAUDE.md's own precedence rule, rewritten to describe this screen's actual states (default/with-image/create-inline/before-confirm/unmapped/confirm-error/confirmed + reopened-after-confirm read-only view). **Verification, real not simulated**: ran `supabase db reset` against a real local Docker Postgres stack — all 45 migrations (0000-0044) applied cleanly including the new one; wrote and ran `packages/db/tests/console_confirm_purchase_invoice.test.ts` (the four cases the WBS prompt's own "Tests" section lists: no-confirm-no-cost-write, unmapped-lines-record-as-expense-only, forced-failure-rolls-back-every-write-including-an-earlier-successful-line-in-the-same-call — using `withRollback`'s savepoint-recovery mechanism to prove this, not just assert it — and a 5-line timing smoke test) — all 4 pass; full `packages/db` suite re-run clean (235 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, zero regressions); `pnpm --filter @brewledger/console typecheck`/`lint` clean (only pre-existing `<img>`-vs-`next/image` warnings, same as 4 other existing console screens); `pnpm lint:boundary` clean; `next build` for `apps/console` succeeds with the new route listed; RL-2 forbidden-phrase grep against `apps/console/` returns nothing. Fixed in `packages/db/migrations/0044_console_confirm_purchase_invoice.sql` (mirrored byte-identical to `supabase/migrations/`, re-verified via diff): added `select ... from purchase_invoices where id = p_invoice_id for update` before the idempotency check — this is the same mechanism `console_advance_order` (0040) gets by delegating to `transition_order`'s own `for update` (0032), taken directly here since 0044 has no such delegate. Also considered and fixed the adjacent race the reviewer flagged for judgment: two *different* invoices confirming concurrently against the *same* ingredient. RL-2 forbidden-phrase grep against `apps/console/` still returns nothing (untouched by this pass). **redline_reviewer targeted re-check**: independently re-verified both `for update` locks sit exactly where they need to (before the idempotency read; before the ingredient cost read/write), reran `supabase db reset` + the concurrency test itself with real separate `pg` connections (not trusting the pasted output), confirmed the two migration files stay byte-identical, and reasoned through the adjacent two-different-invoices/same-ingredient case independently: `stock_ledger` is insert-only (grepped `0017_stock_ledger.sql` — no UPDATE path exists on it anywhere), so there is no read-modify-write on that table for concurrent invoices to corrupt regardless of interleaving; `current_unit_cost_satang`'s final value was already correct without the second lock since Postgres's own implicit UPDATE lock decides last-committer-wins (the documented latest-purchase-price method), and the added lock only removes a stale-read window on the *reported* `oldCostSatang`, which is UI-toast-only and never feeds the `job_queue` payload (confirmed by reading `actions.ts`). (2) the new append-only trigger raised unconditionally on UPDATE/DELETE with no cascade exemption at all, so any `ingredients`/`stores`/`purchase_invoices`/`auth.users` cascade delete touching a row with cost history now hard-failed — reproduced live, it crashed WBS 6.4's own existing concurrency-test cleanup. **engineer fix pass**: restored the missing `stock_ledger` insert (diffed against 0044 line-by-line, confirmed programmatically that exactly the three intended changes remain); rebuilt the trigger using the exact narrow-shape-check pattern this codebase already had to invent twice for `order_status_history` (WBS 5.7's migrations `0034`→too-broad `pg_trigger_depth()>1`, `0035`→narrowed to match the specific cascade shape), adapted for this table's three FKs. Full suite: 249→252 passed, 0 failed. **redline_reviewer full audit** (not fast-path — new table+RLS+trigger, costing-adjacent write path) independently verified the stock_ledger restoration and the trigger narrowing, confirmed RL-1/RL-2/RL-3 posture, but raised **1 HIGH**: the table's three FKs (`ingredient_id`/`store_id` CASCADE, `source_invoice_id` SET NULL, all converging from one `stores` deletion — structurally the same multi-path race 0034/0035 fixed for `order_status_history`, arguably worse with three paths instead of two) were left non-deferrable, applying only the trigger-shape lesson from that precedent and not its `deferrable initially deferred` lesson. **engineer fix pass 2**: reasoned independently from the reviewer's own suggested split (which used a hop-count heuristic) to a mechanism-based one — deferred both CASCADE FKs (`ingredient_id`, `store_id`) as the two possible "victims", left the SET NULL FK (`source_invoice_id`, the "trigger") non-deferred since writing NULL trivially satisfies its own check — and disclosed candidly that the race itself stayed non-reproducible even with the already-proven `order_status_history` fix temporarily reverted and rerun 5×, consistent with the precedent's own description of a genuine but non-deterministic Postgres RI-ordering race. **qa_engineer** added a `pg_constraint` introspection test (deterministic proof the deferral is actually applied, independent of the race) plus a committed-fixture end-to-end cascading-delete regression test, with an explicit header disclosing it cannot force the race either. Full suite: 254 passed, 0 failed. **redline_reviewer targeted re-check**: independently tested the engineer's stated cross-column re-validation mechanism directly against a real Postgres 17 instance (thousands of rows, batched cascading deletes, both this table's topology and the `order_status_history` precedent topology with its fix removed) and **found the stated mechanism does not hold** — an UPDATE on one FK column never re-validates a different FK column on the same row. Recommended marking `done` regardless, since the applied fix (deferring two FKs) is harmless by construction (only delays a check, never weakens one) and the introspection test proves it's genuinely applied — but flagged the migration's own comment for stating a falsified mechanism as fact. **This session corrected the migration comment directly** (both mirrors, re-verified byte-identical) and the corresponding test-file comment, reframing the deferral as "defensive by structural analogy, mechanism unconfirmed" rather than a confirmed causal claim, without touching the schema itself. Re-ran `supabase db reset` (all 46 migrations clean) and the full suite once more post-correction: 254 passed, 0 failed, `check-migration-numbers.mjs` clean. **Zero remaining findings. Marking `done`.** |

Ranks menu items by **total profit contribution** (`revenueSatang - cogsSatang`, both tracked-lines-only per item), the headline MVP KPI per the WBS's own words — not units sold, not revenue. `fetchProfitPerDish.ts` reads `orders` in the selected window filtered to `REVENUE_STATUSES` (served by `orders_store_id_created_at_revenue_status_idx`, 0042), then `order_items` for those `order_id`s (served index-only by `order_items_order_id_covering_idx`, 0042 — already `INCLUDE`s `menu_item_id, quantity`, both money snapshots). **Did not use `order_items_store_id_menu_item_id_idx`** (0042's index built explicitly for this entry): `order_items` carries no `created_at`/date column of its own (`0012_order_items.sql`), so any query filtering by period can only start from `orders`; starting from `order_items` by `store_id` instead would scan every order_item the store ever recorded, not just the period, which is slower for a 30-day report, not faster — documented in the file's own header comment rather than silently diverging from the dispatching session's stated index-shape expectation. **RL-2 item-level bucketing**: an item is "tracked" only if EVERY order_item line sold for it in the period had a non-null `unit_cost_snapshot_satang`; if any line in the period is null, the whole item's cost/margin/profit render `—` and it moves to a separate, neutrally headed section below the ranked list (reuses `UntrackedDisclosure`'s existing `dish` variant verbatim — already built, already matching state_matrix.md's `ยังไม่มีข้อมูลต้นทุน (N รายการ)` copy, not re-authored) — units sold and revenue (both always known) still shown. Uses the frozen `unit_price_snapshot_satang`/`unit_cost_snapshot_satang` snapshot columns only, never `ingredients.current_unit_cost_satang`, so a historical period's figures can't change after a later ingredient cost update (same property WBS 7.5's own row already established for the same columns). **Divergence insight**: computed as best-seller (max units sold, across tracked AND untracked items — units are always known regardless of cost tracking) vs. top profit contributor (max `totalProfitSatang`, necessarily a tracked item); shown only when the two differ, never fabricated when there's no tracked item to compare against (topProfit undefined -> no insight) — matches state_matrix.md's own example sentence verbatim via `insightSentence()`. **Sort**: `ProfitPerDishView` always resets to `totalProfitSatang` descending whenever the `report` object changes (a period change is a fresh load, per the WBS prompt's own "always RETURN to profit-contribution sort on load" instruction) — re-sort by any column is allowed via clickable `<th>`s in between. Fixed per the reviewer's own recommended option (a): `bestSeller`'s reduce now iterates over `tracked` only, so if the true top-seller-by-units is untracked, the insight is suppressed entirely rather than shown with a fabricated pairing — silence is the correct, honest behavior since the code has nothing provable to say about an untracked item's profit. Doc comments on `DivergenceInsight`/`ProfitPerDishReport` and the `bestSeller` reduce itself rewritten to state explicitly that the comparison is "top seller AMONG TRACKED ITEMS", not "top seller overall" — the Thai copy string itself (`copy.ts`'s `insightSentence`) is unchanged, since it's `state_matrix.md`'s own verbatim template with named placeholders and carries no "overall" claim of its own.
