Concrete failure: `tracked=[Mocha(90u,profit200),Latte(50u,profit2000)]`, `untracked=[ColdBrew(1000u)]` — old code picked Mocha as bestSeller (max of tracked only) and fired the insight naming Mocha "ขายดีที่สุด" (an unqualified superlative per `copy.ts`'s verbatim state_matrix.md template, left untouched per the reviewer's own instruction) while ColdBrew genuinely outsold it 1000 to 90 — no invented number, but a false superlative claim, a plausible pilot-shop shape (one popular drink still missing a recipe). Fixed in `fetchProfitPerDish.ts`: now computes `trueOverallBestSeller` as the max-units row across `[...tracked, ...untracked]` (units sold is always known, not fabricated cost data) and only lets the insight proceed when that row's key matches the tracked-only `bestSeller`; otherwise suppresses the insight entirely, matching what the prior doc comment had incorrectly claimed already happened. Traced by hand: the ColdBrew scenario above now suppresses (`trueOverallBestSeller`=ColdBrew, `bestSeller`=Mocha, keys differ → null); the original HIGH scenario (single untracked item outselling a single tracked item) still suppresses correctly (unchanged from round 1); a genuine tracked-best-seller case (tracked item really does have the most units among all rows) still fires the insight normally. Doc comments on `DivergenceInsight` and the `bestSeller`/`trueOverallBestSeller` reduce rewritten to describe this guarantee accurately rather than aspirationally. `npx tsc --noEmit` on `apps/console` shows no new errors referencing this file (same pre-existing `@brewledger/costing/dist/units`/typed-route baseline as round 1). `npx eslint` clean on the touched file. RL-2 forbidden-phrase grep over `apps/console/` still zero matches. `copy.ts` not touched, per the dispatching session's explicit instruction. **Final targeted re-check (redline_reviewer, scoped to `e608e11` only)**: re-derived all four scenarios by hand against the actual code, not narrative — the MEDIUM's ColdBrew case (`trueOverallBestSeller`=ColdBrew, `bestSeller`=Mocha, keys differ → suppressed, confirmed), the original HIGH's scenario (still suppressed, no regression), and a legitimate positive case (tracked item genuinely is the overall max-units row → insight fires correctly, confirming the fix didn't overcorrect into always-suppress). Confirmed both reduces use the same total-order tie-break rule (units desc, Thai locale name asc) so the two reduces can only diverge exactly when suppression is the correct behavior. Doc comments confirmed to now state only what the code actually guarantees. `tsc --noEmit` clean on this file. **Both the original HIGH and the round-1 MEDIUM are genuinely closed, no new issue introduced. If the delivered prototype's overview screen still renders a three-line fee breakdown, remove it"), so nothing in this build renders one — `design/console-reports.js`'s `scOverview()` (the prototype's own dev harness) still renders a `ค่าธรรมเนียมเดือนนี้` card, deliberately not ported. **Aggregation is a pure SUM over `daily_financials`, no live join over `orders`/`order_items`** — matches WBS 7.8's design note that reports should read the pre-aggregated table where possible, and matches this entry's own dispatching instructions. `fetchOverview.ts`'s `aggregateRange()` (pure, unit-tested directly) enumerates every expected `business_date` in the requested range and sums `gross_revenue_satang`/`other_expense_satang`/`order_count` from whatever rows exist, but treats `total_cogs_satang`/`net_profit_satang` as poisoned to `null` for the WHOLE range the moment either (a) any expected date is missing from `daily_financials` (the nightly `dailyAggregate` job — WBS 7.5 — writes a row for every store every night, so a missing row means "not aggregated yet", not "no orders that day") or (b) any present row itself carries a null cost (untracked items that day) — RL-2's null discipline extended from a single day (WBS 7.5's own `fetchPnlDay.ts`) to an arbitrary range, never coerced to 0, never silently dropped from the sum. **Comparison modes** (`periods.ts`): เดือนนี้/เดือนที่แล้ว (default), ปีนี้/ปีที่แล้ว, and a custom range pair (four date fields — current-from/to, previous-from/to) via `<input type=date>`, same client-refetch-on-change posture as WBS 7.6's period chips. Month/year modes compare **like-for-like day counts**: a mid-month "current" period (e.g. Aug 1-16) is compared against the SAME day count of the previous month (Jul 1-16), clamped when the target day doesn't exist in the shorter month (Mar 31 -> Feb 28); year mode is the same math one level up (Jan 1-Aug 16 this year vs Jan 1-Aug 16 last year, clamped for Feb 29). Custom mode never applies this clamp — the merchant picked both ranges explicitly, matching this WBS entry's own "same day count **where relevant**" Acceptance wording. **Zero-baseline and null both client, plus `monthComparisonRanges`/`yearComparisonRanges` like-for-like-day-count and clamping cases — 9/9 passing (`pnpm exec vitest run`). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/console`. `npx eslint` Not tested against a live Postgres/Supabase stack — same standing environment caveat as every other Phase 7 row; the mocked-client shape mirrors WBS 7.5/7.6's already-proven pattern. No qa_engineer leg per Pattern C (screen tests consolidated at WBS 8.2 E2E). **Next: redline_reviewer** — RL-2 null-propagation arithmetic in `aggregateRange`/`fetchMonthlyTrend`, the zero-baseline-vs-null delta distinction, Thai copy against state_matrix.md (including the two newly added entries), the flagged 10px->11px prototype divergence, and confirmation that no fee figure survived anywhere on this screen (RL-1/WBS 4.8).  **Post-review fix (engineer, CRITICAL+HIGH)**: `redline_reviewer`'s first review round blocked sign-off on two findings, both fixed in `49cedc8`. CRITICAL -- `monthComparisonRanges` (`periods.ts`) clamped only the previous month's end date to avoid an invalid date (e.g. Feb 30 -> Feb 28) but never truncated the CURRENT range to match, so a late-month comparison (e.g. Mar 29 vs a 28-day Feb) silently compared unequal day counts while `incomplete` stayed true and the screen kept asserting equal ranges -- recurring on the last 1-3 days of every 31-day month whose predecessor is shorter, not hypothetical. Fixed by truncating `currentTo` to the clamped day whenever the clamp actually engages; added regression tests for Mar 29 (28-day Feb), the Mar 29 leap-year case (29-day Feb, no clamp needed), and a loop over every recurring 31-vs-shorter-month skew date. HIGH -- `RevenueProfitChart.tsx`'s tooltip checked truthiness of the recharts payload entry object rather than its `value`, so hovering an RL-2 untracked-cost month (`netProfitSatang: undefined`) rendered "฿NaN" instead of "—". Fixed by extracting `tooltipMoneyText()` and null-checking the value itself; added a unit test covering the undefined-value case. Re-ran `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (clean on touched files), and the full overview vitest suite: 16/16 passing (12 prior + 4 new). RL-2 forbidden-phrase grep over `apps/console/`: zero matches. Status held at needs review -- sign-off is `redline_reviewer`'s call, not engineer's. **Post-review fix round 3 (engineer, CRITICAL, in `b697306`)**: `49cedc8`'s fix over-corrected -- it truncated `currentTo` whenever `clampedDay < day`, with no check on `incomplete`, so a COMPLETE current month (e.g. 2026-03-31, 31 days) compared against a shorter complete previous month (Feb, 28 days) got silently truncated to `2026-03-01..2026-03-28`, dropping 3 real days of revenue from the `daily_financials` query bound in `fetchOverview.ts`. Worse than the original bug: `incomplete` is correctly `false` here (the current month IS complete), so `OverviewClient.tsx` suppresses the "compared over equal ranges" disclosure note entirely -- the merchant saw an undercounted total with zero caveat. Same pattern hits 2026-05-31, -07-31, -10-31, -12-31 (every 31-day month whose predecessor is shorter). Root cause: `clampedDay < day` alone doesn't distinguish "current month is incomplete and needs truncating to match the previous month's shorter day count" (the real WBS 7.7 requirement) from "current month is complete and simply has a different day count than a shorter previous month" (expected, not a bug -- the "same day range" copy only ever applies to the incomplete-month note). Fixed in `periods.ts`'s `monthComparisonRanges` by gating the truncation on `incomplete && clampedDay < day`, so `currentTo` stays at `today` unconditionally whenever the current month is complete. Verified by hand: 2026-03-31 vs Feb (incomplete=false, clampedDay=28<31, current now stays `2026-03-31` not `2026-03-28`); 2026-03-29 vs Feb (incomplete=true, clampedDay=28<29, current still correctly truncates to `2026-03-28`, round-1 fix intact); 2024-03-29 leap year (incomplete=true, clampedDay=29=day, no clamp fires either way). Also fixed the test gap that hid this: the round-2 skew-date loop test in `fetchOverview.test.ts` gated its own assertion on `if (ranges.incomplete)`, silently skipping every complete-month date where this bug fired -- restructured to assert explicitly on both branches (equal day counts when incomplete, `current.toBusinessDate` exactly equal to the input date with no truncation when complete), plus two new explicit tests for 2026-03-31 and the four other recurring skew dates (May/Jul/Oct/Dec 31). Re-ran `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, full overview vitest suite: 18/18 passing (16 prior + 2 new). RL-2 forbidden-phrase grep over `apps/console/`: zero matches. Status held at needs review -- sign-off is `redline_reviewer`'s call, not engineer's. **Round-3 scoped re-check (redline_reviewer, `b697306`) -- SIGNED OFF.** Re-derived the gating fix independently (not trusted from the commit message) and hand-traced 2026-03-31 (incomplete=false, stays untruncated), 2026-03-29 (incomplete=true, still truncates to match 28-day Feb, round-1 fix intact), and 2024-03-29 leap year (no clamp needed) -- all correct, no regression of round 1 reintroduced, and the same pattern confirmed across the other four recurring skew months (May/Jul/Oct/Dec 31). Independently re-ran the full overview vitest suite rather than trusting the engineer's count: 18 tests pass (not 20 as the engineer's round-3 note originally stated -- a narrative typo, corrected above; the real coverage is exactly what was described qualitatively, two genuinely new assertions plus a restructured existing one, not four). Confirmed the test restructuring asserts both the `incomplete=true` and `incomplete=false` branches explicitly rather than relocating the blind spot. Also caught and closed a second documentation nit independently: the round-3 note as originally written cited pre-amend commit `582cd61`, a dangling object unreachable from any branch or tag (confirmed via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, `git branch --contains`, `git tag --contains` -- all empty) that will not survive garbage collection; corrected to the real final hash `b697306` throughout this row. All three review rounds (round 1: RL-1 fee absence, RL-2 null propagation, Thai copy verbatim match, tap targets, `/design/` untouched, 11px chart-label floor; round 2: chart tooltip null-vs-zero handling; round 3: like-for-like day-count alignment) are closed. Sign-off: **WBS 7.7 done.** |

| 7.8 | Reporting Query Performance and Indexing | 7 | done | A (architect -> engineer -> qa_engineer -> redline_reviewer, then an orchestrator-applied fix round for the reviewer's own fully-specified MEDIUM/LOW) | engineer | redline_reviewer | `4f8625e`, `9cea841`, `8f88629` | Architect designed (design-only pass, no files), engineer implemented per that design without deviation. **Migration** `packages/db/migrations/0042_reporting_indexes.sql`, mirrored byte-identical to `supabase/migrations/0042_reporting_indexes.sql` (confirmed identical via `diff`) — verified `0041` unused in either directory (WBS 6.5 was reverted, `e5de660`) so `0042` is the next free number. Three indexes, each with an inline comment naming the query it serves: `orders_store_id_created_at_revenue_status_idx` (partial, `store_id, created_at desc where status in (...)`, the reporting hot path shared by 7.1/7.5/7.7), `order_items_order_id_covering_idx` (replaces the old `order_items_order_id_idx` — same leading column, now `INCLUDE`s `menu_item_id, quantity, unit_price_snapshot_satang, unit_cost_snapshot_satang` for an index-only scan on the 7.1/7.6 cost rollup), `order_items_store_id_menu_item_id_idx` (7.6 profit-per-dish). Two of the WBS's own six suggested indexes deliberately omitted per the architect's design, both explained in the migration's own header comment: `daily_financials(store_id, business_date desc)` is redundant with the table's existing `unique (store_id, business_date)` btree (0019); `ingredient_cost_history(ingredient_id, changed_at desc)` targets a table that doesn't exist yet (WBS 6.6, unbuilt) — not stubbed. `stock_ledger`'s own index (0017) already covers its case verbatim, no action needed. Used plain `create index` (matches every prior migration in this repo — grepped, zero precedent for `concurrently`), not `create index concurrently`; could not time the build against a live fixture to validate that choice (see environment blocker below) — flagged as a judgment call, not a verified one. **Fixture generator** `packages/db/perf-seed.ts` (+ `perf-seed` script and `tsx` devDependency added to `packages/db/package.json`, `perf-seed.ts` added to `tsconfig.tests.json`'s `include`, `db:perf-seed` convenience script added to the root `package.json`) — 183-day rolling window (~6 months), ~60 orders/day with per-day jitter, 25 menu items (5 with zero `bom_lines`, RL-2), 15 ingredients, ~1 purchase invoice/day (1-4 lines against the 15 ingredients) with matching `stock_ledger` purchase rows, one `daily_financials` row per business date computed from that day's generated orders. Weighted status distribution (~2% `PENDING_PAYMENT` in the last 3 days only, ~3% `EXPIRED`, ~3% `CANCELLED`/`REFUNDED` combined, remainder weighted 80% `COLLECTED`/6% `READY`/7% `PREPARING`/7% `ACCEPTED`) so the new partial index's selectivity is actually exercised. `unit_cost_snapshot_satang` null whenever the sold item has no recipe (deterministic, ~20% of items) plus a further random 5% of tracked-item lines, landing overall null share in the 15-20% band the WBS specifies without hard-coding an exact percentage. Fixed `mulberry32` PRNG seed (`0xb2e57ed6`) for reproducible *choices*; absolute dates are NOT fixed (window is "183 days ending now", a genuinely rolling 6 months per the WBS's own framing) — documented in the file header so a future reader doesn't mistake this for full byte-for-byte reproducibility. Truncate-and-regenerate is scoped to one fixture tree (deletes+cascades from a fixed `auth.users` row, store slug `perf-fixture-store`) rather than `TRUNCATE`-ing whole tables, so it never destroys `packages/db/seed.sql`'s unrelated demo-cafe fixture or other local dev data sharing the same stack — a deliberate, documented narrowing of "truncate-and-regenerate" from the literal SQL verb. Local-only guard mirrors `packages/shared/src/config.ts`'s `assertLocalSupabaseInDev` (refuses any host other than `127.0.0.1`/`localhost`). Batched multi-row inserts (chunks of 500) rather than one round trip per row. **`docs/db/query_plans.md`**: header + fixture-header table + full WBS 7.1 section (all four `fetchDashboardSummary.ts` queries, which index serves each) structured and ready, but every actual `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`/row-count/wall-time cell is marked `(pending)` rather than fabricated — see environment blocker below. WBS 7.5/7.6/7.7 sections present as explicit "pending — WBS not yet implemented" stubs per the dispatching session's own instruction, not fabricated. **CI performance smoke test** `packages/db/tests/perf/report_query_performance.test.ts` — engineer-built harness (per this dispatch's explicit scope: implement the runnable harness, leave validation/extension to `qa_engineer`) covering all four WBS 7.1 queries with real `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` execution, a `Seq Scan` regex assertion against `orders`/`order_items`, a 3s CI-fail `expect().toBeLessThan()`, and a non-failing 2s p95 warning — skips (never fails) when the local stack or the `perf-fixture-store` fixture isn't present, same posture as every other `packages/db/tests/*.test.ts`. WBS 7.5/7.6/7.7 sections are `describe.skip`/`it.todo` stubs, not fabricated coverage. **Environment blocker, confirmed directly this session** (same standing caveat as WBS 7.1's own row and `packages/db/tests/*`): `supabase --version` succeeds (`2.115.0` installed) but `supabase start` was never run — a direct `pg` connect to `127.0.0.1:54322` returned `ECONNREFUSED`. Could not run `perf-seed.ts` against a live database, could not capture real `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` output, could not verify `perf-seed.ts`'s SQL executes without error end-to-end, could not time the plain-`create index` migration to validate the `concurrently`-fallback judgment call above. `npx tsc --noEmit` (via `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db typecheck`, which also runs `tsc -p tsconfig.tests.json` covering `perf-seed.ts` and the new test file) is clean. No `apps/console`/`apps/shop` files touched — RL-2 nag-phrase grep and `apps/shop` bundle scan N/A to this entry. (2) WBS 7.1b (`orders` filtered to the three working statuses) genuinely and reproducibly Seq Scans post-`ANALYZE`, confirmed 3 times, and `set enable_seqscan = off` shows the index-forced plan is not actually faster at this fixture's volume (3.88ms bitmap-heap-scan vs. 3.47ms seq scan, ~18% selectivity, table fits in a few hundred buffers) — **orchestrator decision: accept as a documented exception rather than force the planner**, since forcing it would make the query slower for no benefit. `report_query_performance.test.ts`'s WBS 7.1b test now asserts only the timing budget for this one query (comment explains why, cites the measured numbers, flags re-testing if daily order volume grows ~10x); every other query in the file keeps the strict `assertNoSeqScanOn` check. `docs/db/query_plans.md`'s WBS 7.1b section and its Status header updated to record this as resolved/documented, not an open question. Migration `0042_reporting_indexes.sql` untouched — still correct, will matter at higher order volumes. Re-ran `pnpm db:perf-seed` for real (orders=10900, order_items=26059, purchase_invoices=180, purchase_line_items=454, stock_ledger=454, daily_financials=183 — matches the recorded fixture header exactly) and the full suite: **223 passed, 1 skipped, 3 todo (227 total)**, zero failures. **redline_reviewer** (full Pattern A audit, independent — did not author `4f8625e`/`9cea841`/`8f88629`): re-verified everything independently against a live local Postgres rather than trusting prior legs' reports — byte-identical migration mirror, no numbering collision (`0041` never created, only a harmless gap), the two omitted/deferred indexes and the pre-existing `stock_ledger` index all confirmed correct against the real schema, `perf-seed.ts`'s local-only guard and scoped truncate-and-regenerate confirmed load-bearing by direct testing, independently reran `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db run perf-seed` (exact same counts) and the full suite (**223/1/3/0**, matching), independently re-read the WBS 7.1b Seq Scan exception's reasoning and found it technically sound (standard, well-understood planner cost behavior at this table's real size, not a bug or misconfigured index). **Found 1 MEDIUM + 1 LOW, both non-blocking**: (MEDIUM) `order_items_store_id_idx` (0012) became redundant once `order_items_store_id_menu_item_id_idx` was added — same class of omission the migration itself had already correctly avoided for `order_items_order_id_idx`; | 6.9 | Cost per Cup and Profit Recalculation — cross-session merge fix (0048) | 6 | done | B (engineer -> redline_reviewer, full audit; qa-equivalent regression test added directly by the orchestrating session) | engineer | redline_reviewer | uncommitted | Found while merging WBS 6.7 (this session's own work) into `origin/main`, which had already received this entry: **independently reproduced on a clean `origin/main` checkout with zero WBS 6.7 code present**, so this is 6.9's own pre-existing defect, not an interaction with 6.7. `0046`'s `trg_bom_lines_cost_recalc` trigger fires `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE ON bom_lines` and unconditionally `INSERT`s into `menu_item_cost_cache` — including on a DELETE that arrives via cascade (`delete from auth.users` -> merchants -> stores -> menu_items/bom_lines), at which point the row's own `menu_item_id`/`store_id` may already be gone from the SAME cascade, raising `menu_item_cost_cache_menu_item_id_fkey`. This broke `packages/db/tests/order_lifecycle.test.ts` and `payment_confirmation.test.ts`'s standard `afterAll` cleanup outright — a foundational pattern nearly every DB test in this repo relies on. Same bug class this repo already hit and fixed twice this session (`order_status_history` 0034/0035, `ingredient_cost_history` 0045), but the correct fix here is different: those were raw FK constraints fixable by deferring the check to end-of-transaction; here the parent is genuinely, permanently gone once the enclosing transaction commits, so deferring would only move the failure to COMMIT — the actual fix is a guard *before* the INSERT. New migration `packages/db/migrations/0048_menu_item_cost_cache_cascade_guard.sql` (mirrored byte-identical, does **not** touch `0046` — that migration is already committed on `origin/main`): `CREATE OR REPLACE` of `enqueue_menu_item_cost_recalc()` adding `if not exists (select 1 from menu_items where id = v_menu_item_id) or not exists (select 1 from stores where id = v_store_id) then return coalesce(NEW, OLD); end if;` before the cache upsert/job enqueue. **FK topology analysis, independently verified by redline_reviewer against the actual migration files** (not assumed): checking `menu_items` alone is insufficient — `bom_lines` carries its own direct `store_id -> stores` cascade FK, a sibling path to `bom_lines -> menu_items -> stores`, and Postgres gives no ordering guarantee between sibling RI cascade actions from one `stores` deletion, so both parents need checking. Verified the guard does not weaken the trigger's normal (non-cascade) behavior — every ordinary `bom_lines` insert/update/delete already satisfies `bom_lines`' own `NOT NULL` + FK guarantees on those same columns, so the guard is a no-op on any path that isn't a same-transaction cascade. Concurrent (cross-transaction, non-cascade) deletion of the parent is not a gap either — Postgres's standard FK row-locking at insert-time already serializes that case correctly; the guard only covers the same-transaction-cascade command-counter visibility gap the raw FK check couldn't see. **Regression test added** (`packages/db/tests/order_lifecycle.test.ts`, new "WBS 6.9 follow-up (0048)" describe block, mirroring the existing 0034 cascade-delete test's pattern exactly): one test proves-nonzero-then-cascade-deletes-then-proves-zero across `stores`/`menu_items`/`bom_lines`/`menu_item_cost_cache`/`job_queue`, with the trigger confirmed `tgenabled = 'O'` before and after (no `DISABLE TRIGGER` workaround); a second test proves the guard doesn't silently break the trigger's real job — a direct, non-cascade `bom_lines` delete still marks the cache row stale and enqueues a `cost_recalc` job. Also fixed, mechanical fallout of the merge: `packages/db/tests/rls.test.ts`'s pinned table count (23->24, `menu_item_cost_cache` added to the name list — this session's own WBS 6.6 had already bumped it 22->23 for `ingredient_cost_history`, done independently by whichever session landed 6.9 second); `apps/console/src/costRenderingAudit.test.ts`'s `MANIFEST` extended with `menu/[id]/MenuItemEditorForm.tsx` (WBS 6.7's own live cost preview, absent from 6.9's manifest since it was written before 6.7 existed on either branch — verified null-safe, no coercion, already redline-reviewed under WBS 6.7 itself); and one pre-existing lint **error** (not a warning) on `origin/main` itself, unrelated to either entry — `ProfitPerDishClient.tsx`'s teaching-default sort reset ran `setSort(...)` synchronously inside a bare `useEffect`, which a newer `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` rule (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`) now flags; moved to React's documented "adjust state during render" pattern (compare `report` against a `prevReport` state slot, no behavior change, verified via the full console suite still green). **redline_reviewer**: independently confirmed every FK cited against the actual migration files (`0005_menu_items.sql`, `0009_bom_lines.sql`, `0046_menu_item_cost_cache.sql`), confirmed 0048's scope is `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` only (no new table/trigger, `0046` untouched), ran `supabase db reset` (48 migrations clean) and `pnpm --filter @brewledger/db test` independently (254/254 before the regression test existed, then 256/256 after), confirmed byte-identical mirrors, confirmed `check-migration-numbers.mjs` clean. Raised one MEDIUM (no committed test existed yet for the cascade path — engineer had verified by hand only) — closed directly by the orchestrating session with the regression test described above rather than a separate qa_engineer dispatch, given the pattern to mirror (0034's own test) was fully specified by the finding itself. **Zero remaining findings. Marking `done`.** |

The one failure was diagnosed precisely as a bug in the new test's own transaction handling, not the migration: the "does NOT block the real cascade" test's role-switch (`set_config(..., true)`/`set local role authenticated`) is transaction-scoped and was reverted before the next query ran because that one test (uniquely, correctly) skips the file's usual `withRollback` wrapper so its `auth.users` delete can actually commit. **Engineer fix round 1**: wrapped just that test's role-switch + `console_confirm_payment`/`console_confirm_invoice` calls in an explicit `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`; re-ran — 352/352 (`packages/db` 264 + `packages/costing` 88), no regressions. **redline_reviewer round 1** (full audit, RL-2 touch, no fast path): confirmed byte-identical migration mirrors, confirmed the DELETE/UPDATE exemption shapes correct for all four FKs against the actual precedent files, confirmed the negative-stock policy documentation and Thai copy match `state_matrix.md` exactly, confirmed `ingredient_stock_levels()`'s `coalesce(...,0)` makes `isNegativeStock`'s non-nullable signature safe. **Found 3 findings**: [HIGH] the one cascade test could not distinguish "the order_id/purchase_invoice_id SET NULL exemption branch fired and was exempted" from "the branch never ran because the row was already gone via the store_id/ingredient_id DELETE branch racing ahead of it" — no isolated test proved those two UPDATE branches actually execute; [MEDIUM] `0049`'s header claim "enforced not merely intended" didn't scope that row-level triggers never fire on `TRUNCATE`, nor that the worker's direct `DATABASE_URL` credential (table-owner privilege, documented elsewhere as bypassing RLS) could defeat it via `TRUNCATE`/`ALTER TABLE...DISABLE TRIGGER` — an inherited, unaddressed gap shared by the `order_status_history`/`ingredient_cost_history` precedents too, not unique to this migration; [LOW] `packages/costing/src/index.ts`'s comment overstated `isNegativeStock` as accepting `number | null` when it only accepts `number`. **Fix round 2, dispatched in parallel** (tests to qa_engineer, comments to engineer, per this repo's standing "engineer doesn't write its own tests" rule): qa_engineer added two isolated tests (delete only an order, store/ingredient left intact so no other FK branch can race — asserts the delete doesn't raise and the referencing row survives with `order_id` now null, plus a negative control against a genuinely different order's untouched row; same shape for `purchase_invoice_id`) — 10/10 in the file, 266/266 in the full `packages/db` suite, no regressions. Engineer added a scoping caveat to `0049`'s header (PostgREST-grant roles only; `TRUNCATE`/`DISABLE TRIGGER`/the direct DB credential explicitly out of scope, same accepted posture as the two precedent tables) mirrored byte-identically into both migration copies, and corrected the `index.ts` comment; `tsc --noEmit` on `packages/costing` clean. **redline_reviewer round 2** (targeted re-check, not a full re-audit): read both new tests directly and re-ran the file against real Postgres itself rather than trusting the report (10/10 pass) — confirmed the isolation claim holds structurally, since a broken exemption would make the `DELETE FROM orders`/`purchase_invoices` statement itself raise, not just leave stale data, and the negative controls reference genuinely distinct rows; confirmed the `diff` between both `0049` copies is empty; confirmed the `index.ts` comment now matches `stock.ts`'s real signatures and `tsc --noEmit` is clean. **All three findings RESOLVED. Zero remaining findings. Marking `done`.** Not yet committed — orchestrating session's call on when to commit. |

| 5.11 | Order Cancellation and Merchant-Initiated Refund | 5 | done | B, upgraded to A for the schema/RPC leg per `docs/design/gaps.md` GAP-9's explicit "owned by architect, not engineer unprompted" instruction (architect -> engineer -> qa_engineer -> engineer fix round -> redline_reviewer, full audit, no fast path — RL-1 primary enforcement point, schema, and Customer Web all in scope) | engineer, engineer (fix round) | architect, qa_engineer, redline_reviewer | uncommitted | **RL-1's core framing, restated by the WBS itself**: automatic gateway refunds are impossible (Brew Ledger never held the money), so the merchant transfers the refund from their own banking app and this entry's whole job is making that obligation impossible to lose track of, not moving money. Most of the state machine already shipped under WBS 5.7/6.8/5.12 (`transition_order()` already handled CANCELLED transitions, compensating `stock_ledger` rows, slot release, and `refund_status='pending'`) — this entry's true scope was narrow: a reason column, two resolution columns, two thin wrapper RPCs, and a widened public contract. **Architect** (`docs/db/wbs_5_11_refund_design.md`): `orders.cancel_reason text` (5 fixed check-constraint codes, no free text — mirrors `payment_confirmed_by`'s pattern, not threaded through `transition_order()` itself since `console_undo_cash_sale`'s own CANCELLED call site has no reason to give); `orders.refund_resolved_by uuid references merchants(id)` + `refund_resolved_at timestamptz` (proper FK, deliberately not `payment_confirmed_by`'s legacy `text` cast); partial index `orders_refund_pending_idx` for the never-ages-out รอคืนเงิน list; `console_cancel_order`/`console_resolve_refund` (security definer, `authenticated`-only, BL001 re-read-and-compare idempotency per `wbs_5_7_lifecycle_design.md` §10, structured `{ok:false,code:'ILLEGAL_TRANSITION'}` rather than `raise`); `public_order_status`/`public_order_lookup` widened via DROP+CREATE (not REPLACE — Postgres rejects widening a `RETURNS TABLE` list) to add `cancel_reason`/`refund_status`, with the explicit load-bearing rule that `refund_status`, never `cancel_reason`'s mere presence, may gate refund-claim copy (a `PENDING_PAYMENT` cancel has a reason but owes nothing). **Engineer** implemented verbatim: migration `0051_console_cancel_order_refund.sql` (mirrored byte-identical), `console-cancel-order`/`console-resolve-refund` Edge Functions, console UI (`CancelOrderSheet.tsx` two-step reason-picker-then-confirm per `interaction_spec.md:18`, `RefundPendingSection.tsx`/`fetchRefundPending.ts` for the รอคืนเงิน section, wired the previously-stubbed `ยกเลิกออเดอร์` button on `/console/orders/{id}`), customer tracking page (`apps/shop/.../o/[code]/page.tsx`) restoring the reason/refund-timeframe copy GAP-9 had withheld, and `packages/shared/src/serializers/public.ts`/`public.schema.ts` widening (`.strict()`, so a stray `refundResolvedBy` leak fails parsing). Two disclosed, justified divergences from the prototype's verbatim copy (red-line corrections, precedence rank 1 over rank 5 per CLAUDE.md): the confirm-sheet body no longer implies an automatic system-triggered refund (contradicts RL-1), and cancelled vs. refunded orders get visually distinct cards instead of reusing one in-progress card for both. **QA** (`packages/db/tests/console_cancel_order_refund.test.ts`, 18 new tests) found one genuine regression before engineer's fix round: migration 0051's `public_order_lookup` DROP+CREATE was based on the pre-WBS-5.10 function shape and silently reverted `0037_normalize_order_lookup_phone.sql`'s `+66` phone normalization, breaking real customer lookups for any human-typed phone shape other than the exact stored string — caught by the existing `order_tracking.test.ts` phone-normalization suite, fixed by restoring 0037's `plpgsql` body with the two new columns added on top, re-verified (full `packages/db` suite: 300 passed, 1 skipped, 3 todo). **Redline** full audit: RL-1 confirmed (no gateway call, no balance/escrow/wallet table anywhere, `console_resolve_refund` only ever self-reports resolution); BL001 idempotency confirmed genuine re-read-and-compare in both RPCs (not a blanket already:true); `PENDING_PAYMENT` cancel confirmed to leave `refund_status` null and stay out of the รอคืนเงิน list; RL-3 confirmed (`refund_resolved_by`/`refund_resolved_at` absent from both widened RPCs and rejected by the `.strict()` schema, proven by a `hasOwnProperty` test, not just null); refund-timeframe gating on `refund_status` (never `cancel_reason`) confirmed in both `apps/shop` and console; RL-2 forbidden-phrase grep clean; `stock_ledger`/`transition_order()` confirmed untouched by this entry. Found and closed directly by the orchestrating session (not re-dispatched, both mechanical): `supabase/functions/_tests/order_tracking_http.test.ts`'s hardcoded RL-3 allow-listed-keys assertion was missing the two new keys (its `packages/db/tests/` siblings had already been updated by qa_engineer, this one live-HTTP test was the one file qa's dispatch scope didn't reach) — updated to expect `cancelReason`/`refundStatus` alongside the original five; this row itself, since PROGRESS.md had no entry for 5.11 despite the convention every other entry follows. | the actual code and commit, not just the commit message, before writing this row. **What exists**: `console_advance_order(p_order_id, p_to)` (`supabase/migrations/0040_console_advance_order.sql`, mirrored byte-identical in `packages/db/migrations/`) — a `security definer` wrapper around WBS 5.7's `transition_order()`, needed because `transition_order` itself is revoked from `authenticated` (service_role-only, reached only via a nested call from another security-definer wrapper — same posture as `console_confirm_payment`), so no console Edge Function could call it directly. `p_to` is allow-listed to exactly `PREPARING`/`READY`/`COLLECTED` — CANCELLED/REFUNDED stay WBS 5.11's own exclusive territory (not yet built), an explicit design boundary in the migration's own header comment. Ownership check (NOT_FOUND vs FORBIDDEN, distinguishable, mutates nothing on either) before the transition, actor resolved via `auth_merchant_id()` (the caller's own JWT), matching every other console-* wrapper's pattern. Idempotency deviation from the 0033 precedent, disclosed in the migration's own comment: because this one wrapper covers three from-\>to pairs behind one signature and backs the bulk action below, a genuine illegal-transition catch (BL001 that isn't "already at target") returns `{ok:false, code:'ILLEGAL_TRANSITION'}` (409 at the Edge Function) instead of re-raising as a hard error, with the underlying detail still surfaced via `RAISE WARNING` so it's distinguishable in logs from the benign double-tap race — this was itself a redline_reviewer finding folded into the same commit, per the commit message. Two Edge Functions: `console-advance-order` (single-order transition) and `console-bulk-ready-orders` ("ทำเสร็จทั้งช่วงเวลานี้" — walks each order's own current status and only the hops it actually needs, e.g. ACCEPTED needs two hops to READY while PREPARING needs one; processes each order as its own independent RPC call/transaction, explicitly not one outer transaction, so one failure doesn't block or roll back the rest — matches the WBS's own "atomic per order, honest partial failure" acceptance criterion). Frontend: `orders/actions.ts`'s `advanceOrder`/`bulkMarkReady` (Server Actions bridging to the two Edge Functions via the merchant's forwarded session JWT, same `postConsoleFunction` pattern as `confirmPayment`/`rejectPayment`; a 409 response maps to a specific "this order's status already changed" Thai copy (`ADVANCE_STALE`) rather than the generic failure message), `InboxClient.tsx` (optimistic status overrides keyed by order id, reconciled once the real feed — realtime, 10s poll, or the response itself — confirms the target, or rolled back visibly on failure) and the new `orders/[id]/OrderDetailClient.tsx` + `orders/[id]/page.tsx` (order detail screen with the same action buttons). Action-button labels (เริ่มทำ/พร้อมรับ/รับแล้ว) match the WBS prompt's own Thai copy exactly. **Test coverage confirmed by direct read**: `packages/db/tests/console_advance_order.test.ts` (461 lines) — legal forward chain with correct side effects at each hop, `p_to` outside the allow-list rejected before any lookup (including for a real, owned order), illegal hops (skipping PREPARING, advancing a terminal COLLECTED order, advancing a CANCELLED order) all return `ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` rather than crashing, NOT_FOUND vs FORBIDDEN distinguishable and FORBIDDEN mutates zero rows, same-target double-tap idempotent, and a 10-genuinely-concurrent-connections test on the same hop proving exactly one real transition. `InboxClient.test.tsx` and `actions.test.ts` (frontend, optimistic/rollback and bridge-function behavior) both exist alongside the production files, added in the same commit. **Review, per the commit message** (not re-run by this session — this is a backfill of prior work, not a new review pass): architect confirmed the migration design sound; qa_engineer ran the full local suite live (527 passed, 11 pre-existing unrelated failures); redline_reviewer found no CRITICAL/HIGH (RL-2 copy, Thai copy fidelity, 56px tap targets, transitionOrder-only writes, and bulk-action honesty all confirmed) and two MEDIUM/LOW findings (the illegal-transition RAISE WARNING logging above, and a stale test-coverage comment correction) were folded directly into the same commit rather than a separate fix round. **Not independently re-verified by this session** — no live Postgres run, no re-read of the qa_engineer/redline_reviewer transcripts (they predate this session); this row is a documentation backfill based on the commit message's own claims plus this session's direct read of the actual migration/Edge Function/frontend/test files, not a fresh audit. If that independent re-verification is wanted, treat it as a small follow-up dispatch, not implied by this row. **Marking `done`**, matching the commit's own claimed review outcome. |
